## Port Nyanzaru/Tiryki Anchorage

Tiryki Anchorage  was a ward of the Chultan city of Port Nyanzaru located on the southeast side of the city, outside of its walls. It was considered a slum, and was home to the city's animal-handlers, river folk, and smugglers.

Geography
The anchorage sat at the mouth of the River Tiryki, hence its name.

Description
Tiryki Anchorage hugged the outside of eastern walls of Port Nyanzaru and extended south where it turned into the pens for the city's dinosaurs. The northern and eastern parts of Tiryki Anchorage featured the docks. Much of the district smelled of sweat and sewage, and the odor of dinosaur manure, wet fur, and tanned hides stretched all the way from the dinosaur pens to the walls of the city.

Buildings in Tiryki Anchorage were largely shanties and hovels made of weathered wood and stone, alongside many market tents and some taller structures. They were connected by filthy stone streets, winding and narrow dirt paths, and dark alleyways. Housing was cheap and sometimes very seedy. The district further boasted a large number of taverns, especially around its ramshackle harbor.

Trade
Much of Port Nyanzaru's trade in beasts—notably dinosaurs—occured in Tiryki Anchorage, as did the training of said beasts. By law, both the sale and training of beasts fell under the purview of the merchant prince Ifan Talro'a, and so all legal merchants of beasts were in his employ. There were dozens of these sellers, most of whom doubled as the prince's spies.

The docks of Tiryki Anchorage were virtually unregulated and unpatrolled compared to those of the city's Harbor Ward, and so were quite popular—especially among smugglers—despite the relative lack of resources to help with loading or unloading ships. Those wishing to circumvent the trade monopolies of Port Nyanzaru's merchant princes could find black markets in Tiryki Anchorage. Finding a black market buyer or seller was as simple as asking the right questions to the right people, and it usually took about a half day to track down a new blackmarketeer. However, black market dealings were harshly punished, so any such venture was risky.

Lodging and accommodations were cheaper in Tiryki Anchorage than in most other parts of Port Nyanzaru, and so were popular among visiting explorers.

Defenses
Because it lay outside of Port Nyanzaru's defensive walls, the people of Tiryki Anchorage relied on the volunteer Citizens' Brigade to keep them safe. In case of attack, a horn was sounded to usher the inhabitants to safety and the residents could rely on the keen senses of the many penned beasts in the district to detect invaders. However, unlike Port Nyanzaru's other slum districts, the Anchorage had no safe havens in which to hide (such as the stone ziggurats of the Old City or the Temple of Tymora in Malar's Throat), and so their only recourse was to flee to through Tiryki Gate before it was locked.

Notable Locations
Dinosaur Pens The paddocks to the south were where the city's beasts of burden and racing dinosaurs alike were trained and housed at night. The air was filled with the throaty calls of the huge beasts alongside the general noises and stenches of ranching.
Docks The docks of the anchorage were much less regulated than those located in the Harbor Ward. There was no lighthouse to help guide ships safely into port.
Serpent's Coil This smugglers' tunnel could be accessed via a grate in the west end of the docks, and led into Port Nyanzaru proper.
Tiryki Gate The main access point into the city, connecting the Anchorage to the Market Ward.

Inhabitants
Tiryki Anchorage was home to some of Port Nyanzaru's poorer residents, including animal-handlers and river folk, along with the more rowdy individuals such as smugglers, adventurers, and explorers.

Notable Inhabitants
 Jailo Mek, a smuggler and dinosaur handler.

History
During the colonial rule of Amn over Port Nyanzaru, the Old City was among the districts where people had to live if they did not work for a major trading company.

During the events of the death curse in the late 15 century DR, Tiryki Anchorage was briefly overrun by the forces of the infernal pirate captain Idrianne Smoke.

## Potion of spell enhancement

A potion of spell enhancement was a potion that increased the power of the caster's spells.

Powers
When a mage consumed such a potion, the spells they cast became much more powerful. This potion, however, only lasted for a short duration.

History
In 1358 DR, a chamber to the southwest of Leilon was highly guarded by undead such as spectres and wights . The undead guardians appeared to be protecting a potent potion of spell enhancement.

## P'oh

P'oh were small yet powerful lesser spirits native to Kara-Tur.

Description
P'oh were short and stocky. They had long hair that was usually held back in a ponytail. Their skin was bronze in color. They had a flat nose and large ears. Their clothing was often made from the best silk, garishly decorated with gold and silver threads. P'oh were always males.

Personality
P'oh were arrogant and obnoxious. They were most often seen bearing a condescending grin.

Abilities
The physical touch of a p'oh was deadly, desiccating an opponent. P'oh had the innate ability to duplicate the effects of an invisibility spell at will, and create drought once per week. P'oh used this ability to create drought conditions in an area with a radius of up to . The drought continued until either the p'oh cancelled the spell, was slain, or an ice blight spell was cast.

Combat
P'oh avoided combat whenever they could. Their preferred tactic was to relentlessly taunt an opponent. If confronted by a superior foe, they did not hesitate to retreat. When they had no other choice but to engage in physical combat, they were fierce fighters and showed no mercy. P'oh used a variety of weapons, from simple items such as a strong stick to crafted weapons such as a katana or wakizashi.

Society
P'oh were greatly feared by the inhabitants of Kara-Tur. Villages erected shrines to p'oh along roads, in order to appease them and prevent the p'oh from creating a drought in their area, destroying the villages' crops. When a p'oh decided to settle in an area, they appeared before the local peasants, informing them of the honor they were granted by living near him. He then demanded weekly offerings of large quantities of water or wine as well as fruits and vegetables. If the locals failed to make these offerings or they were deemed insufficient, the angered p'oh would unleash a drought on the area, only lifting it when appeased or killed. On occasion, p'oh were commanded by the Celestial Emperor to punish the inhabitants of a village with their drought ability.
Diet
P'oh were able to consume almost anything and were notorious for drinking massive quantities of liquid in a short period of time.
Homelands
P'oh were nomadic, solitary spirits. They wandered the countryside during day or night. They were most often seen wandering agricultural plains and hills away from large cities. They sometimes made lairs within caves or hollow trees.

## Polluter

Polluter was a satellite of the moon Mingabwe and also considered one of Glyth's three moons.

Description
Polluter was a spherical size-A earth body, which meant that it was less than  in radius. The tiny, rocky worldlet was covered in craters from meteor strikes. Frozen water could be found on its surface, but it had no atmosphere at all. The surface or interior had never been mapped out or mined.

History
The paladin Justin Demonslayer, a former space pirate and resident of Mingabwe, abandoned two of his former magical items somewhere on Polluter so that they could no longer be used for evil.

## Porios

Porios was a mage-for-hire who operated throughout the Sword Coast during the mid–14 century DR.

Description
Porios was something of a pragmatist rather than idealist.

History
During the, Porios was under the employ of the Bhaalspawn Sarevok Anchev during his attempted coup in Baldur's Gate. After Sarevok's death, Porios stole his sword of chaos from the Ducal Palace under the direction of the Bhaalspawn's loyal disciple Korlasz.

Soon after, Porios and his crew were caught by the Hero of Baldur's Gate.

Potions of invulnerability were magic potions of warding.

Description
Potions of invulnerability were typically housed within an iron flask. The syrupy concoction within these potions resembled liquefied iron  and had a tart taste to it. The potion exuded a strange odor. It was so strong that it could be smelled coming from a tightly-sealed vial.

Powers
Upon drinking a potion of invulnerability, a creature would be rendered resistant to all forms of damage for the following minute.

VariantsPaste of invulnerability: The people of Serôs had a thick, gelatinous paste version of this potion, suitable for use in their underwater environment. Like other pastes of Serôs, a paste of invulnerability consisted of kelp and other viscous substances. It was stored in either clamshells or flexible tubes made from eelskin.

History
According to legends the potion, was first concocted for use by a king named Thorkat.

## Point Dawn

Point Dawn was the easternmost fortress in the chain of the Citadel of the Raven complex. It was named for the fact that it was the first part of the Citadel to feel the sun's rays in the morning.

Description
The fortress was dank and uncomforable.

Inhabitants
At maximum capacity the fortress could accommodate five hundred soldiers.

## Point Dusk

Point Dusk was the westernnmost fortress in the chain of the Citadel of the Raven complex and thus the closest part of the Citadel to the setting sun.

Inhabitants
The fortress held two hundred defenders. Soldiers were usually stationed here as a form of punishment for an indiscretion.

Rumors & Legends
The soldiers at Point Dusk whispered that the ghost of Galauntar Hawkhelm haunted the fortress.

## Podokesaurus

Podokesaurus  was a species of dinosaur.
Description
Podokesaurs were small and looked like their cousins compsognathus.
Combat
Podokesaurs were agile predators, striking first and fast. They hunted in packs and a pack of pdokesaurs were able to bring down larger prey.
Habitat
Podokesaurs were most often encountered in forests and plains.
Biology
Podokesaurs were carnivores and lived, and hunted, in packs. If food was scarce, it was not uncommon for podokesaurs to resort to cannibalism, eating weaker members of their pack.

## Poison (rune)

Poison was a type of giant rune that allowed the caster to detect poison in liquids.

Effects
If an inscribed vessel came into contact with any form of poison it would instantly burst and shatter, thereby alerting those nearby to the poison's presence.

Components
Like most giant runes, it required no material components to cast. The process of shaping this rune could take anywhere from one to four hours. The rune needed to be inscribed upon a liquid-bearing vessel, such as a plate, dish, bowl, mug, or glass.

Usage
The poison rune was known to be used extensively by many giant leaders, as they wanted to remain vigilant against any chance of treachery.

## Pooky

Pooky was an imp who served as the familiar of the mage Amon during the mid–14 century DR.

Powers
Pooky was a competent spellcaster.

Relationships
The imp liked to poke fun at Amon's companion Smaeluv Orcslicer by referring to him as "Smelly". Smaeluv in turn wanted to respond with violence.

Pooky saw Amon as a sort of paternal figure, regularly referring to him as "Papa".

## Polish

Polish was a useful cantrip that apprentice wizards could learn. It improved the smoothness of the surfaces of various materials, enhancing their luster.

Effects
This cantrip could be applied to ceramic, crystal, leather, metal, stone, or wood surfaces. Up to  could be affected, but the smaller the area (such as a pair of boots, a mirror, or a single piece of furniture), the more effective the spell. The target had to be fairly clean for this cantrip to work, so the clean cantrip was often employed before polish. After casting, the surface gleamed with improved reflectivity and/or luster. Like similar cantrips, the target had to be within  of the caster.

Components
This cantrip required verbal and somatic components to cast. The caster hummed a little tune while making circular motions with an open palm.

## Poltergeist

Poltergeists were invisible undead creatures.

Description
Poltergeists were invisible to all creatures except those who had the ability to see invisible creatures and objects. When visible, a poltergeist looks like a human clothed in tattered rags and  wrapped with heavy chains, upon which were attached items that represented the evil actions the poltergeist committed when alive.

Personality
Poltergeists were malicious and wicked. Their sole purpose was to inflict suffering on the living. This undying, destructive rage was fueled by their confusion over having no sense of how they had died.

Abilities
Poltergeists projected an aura of fear around them. Unlike most undead, poltergeists were immune to holy water.

Combat
Poltergeists harassed the living by throwing objects at them. If a person was hit with such an object, they were not harmed. A poltergeist could only be hurt or killed by magical weapons or a weapon made from silver. Holy symbols of good-aligned clerics temporarily drove a poltergeist from the area.

Society
Poltergeists were the restless souls of beings who committed evil  deeds when alive, deeds for which they were never brought to justice for. There were two types of poltergeist, those who were forced to wander about and those who were tied to a specific area. Poltergeists who were bonded to an area lived there when alive, while those who were wanderers were killed away from their home, and were forced to wander endlessly, unable to return to their home. Poltergeists who were bonded to an area were much stronger than the wandering variety, and were thus more difficult to dispel or kill.

They did not require nourishment nor did they hoard treasure from their victims.
Habitats
Poltergeists could be found throughout Faerûn. They were encountered in Icewind Dale, Orvaskyte Keep, the Rat Hills, Ravens Bluff, Solon, Undermountain, and Waterdeep.

Beyond the Prime Material plane, poltergeists could be found in the Domains of Dread.

Relationships
Poltergeists were one of many types of undead known to act as servants of Velsharoon.

Poisons and Known Antidotes was a book cataloging poisons and antidotes published before 1269 DR.

History

A copy of this book was found in the Accursed Tower of Icewind Dale in 1369 DR by adventurers searching for the Diary of Damien Morienus. The book was hidden in the secret treasure room inside the tower's library. That copy of Poisons and Known Antidotes was turned into a trap for greedy looters. The insides of the book were hollow with a giant spider hiding inside in temporal stasis. Once the book was opened the spider was enlarged and animated to attack the thieves.

## Poisonous toad

Poisonous toads (or poisonous frogs — the terms were more or less interchangeable) were a species of giant toad whose bodies secreted a weak, hemotoxic poison.

Description
These creatures were largely indistinguishable from the most common, mundane species of giant toad. Like any species of toad, their skin was consistently dry, rough to the touch, and marked by many warts.

Those referred to as "frogs" typically were smaller in size.

Biology
By biting creatures, these toads passed on a poison that sent victims into a comatose state. Their victims would die within twenty-four hours unless treated. Prior to the Time of Troubles, scholars had believed that the poison of their bite was far deadlier.

Combat
Besides their poisonous bite, these toads fought with their tongues like any other.

Ecology
These creatures lived in colonies of up to eight members.

Diet
Poisonous toads were a carnivorous species.

Habitats
Poisonous toads typically inhabited regions with a warm to temperate climate, sometimes living underground. Like any species of giant toad, they lived near bodies of water where they could lay their eggs.

On Toril they were known to inhabit the marshes of Cormyr, including the Farsea Marshes, Marsh of Tun, and Vast Swamp.

## Polyandrium

The Polyandrium was a graveyard outside of Myth Drannor that was the site of an ancient battle between the forces of the invading orc horde and the defenders of Myth Drannor. The most notable feature of The Polyandrium was the monument commemorating the battle.

Geography
The Polyandrium was located to the north from Myth Drannor, and further north-east from the graveyard stood the Shrine of Mystra. The north-western area of the Polyandrium crawled up a low hill. The central part of the site was speckled with shrubs.

The monument erected after that battle was situated in the middle of the clearing that was the Polyandrium. The memorial's base stone was carved out of a white-colored stone and rose  up and was a  square. From the base, three tall marble warrior statues rose up, an elf, a dwarf, and a human. The trio was positioned in a triangle facing outwards with their weapons drawn.

The graveyard held a few larger tombs, big enough to serve full generations of the family, and a dozen of smaller-sized mausoleums that were built for individuals. Numerous grave markers littered the outskirts of the Polyandrium and stretched into the forest surrounding the site. Most of the tombs in the Polyandrium were protected by a combination of a glyph unique to the sealed area, and a word of power, unique to each glyph.

The Tower of Bones stood to the north from the monument, overlooking the Polyandrium. The tower was built, with many protests and outrage, during the Myth Drannor's golden age by necromancers who came to the city to study their art. The Tower of Bones' position outside of the city guaranteed the civil discourse and avoidance of conflicts.

History
The word "polyandrium" meant "the gathering place of fallen warriors". The name was given to this area following an ancient battle between the defenders of Myth Drannor and an orcish horde. The attackers maneuvered the defenders down one of the nearest hills and surrounded them spilling the blood of many valiant warriors. Eventually, the defenders of Myth Drannor persevered.

The Polyandrium became a site of the mass grave for the unidentified fallen defenders. Orcish fallen received a mass grave of their own not war from there. The Myth Drannor erected a monument on the site and with time, the area started being built up with tombs and mausoleums, becoming the secondary graveyard for Myth Drannor.

In 1369 DR, during the Cult of the Dragon's occupation of the ruins of Myth Drannor, one of the Cult's cells was stationed out of the Tower of Bones in the Polyandrium. The Cultists used slave labor and the undead created using expired slaves and enemies to excavate the tombs on the graveyard. By the time a group of heroes pursuing the Cult's slavers arrived at the Polyandrium, about half of the tombs have been broken open and desecrated.

Notable Locations

Candledance Way, the path that connected the Polyandrium and Castle Cormanthor. The road led south, connected to a bridge over a stream, then veered south-west, intersecting with the Street of the Evening Star. The path from there led towards the caste and reached Shaundakul's Throne.
The Tower of Bones, the center for necromantic research, later taken over by the Cult of the Dragon.
Crypt of Kesefehon, the final resting place of the disgraced elven hero Kesefehon.
Crypt of Orbakh, a large crypt decorated with carvings of skulls and runes with its door magically fused with the rock that was the entrance to the crypt.
Crypt of Rothilion, a large tomb that belonged to Rothilion the Honorable, Wizard, and Judge of Myth Drannor.
Ikeshia's Crypt, the tomb of a rich elven philanthropist.
Lyssic's Crypt, the large tomb that housed the dead body of a human commander of Myth Drannor's armies, Lyssic, and his army of clay warrior constructs.
Loresinger's Crypt, the smaller-sized mausoleum for an unnamed musician.

Inhabitants
In 1369 DR, the area was under the control of the Cult of the Dragon, they filled the graveyard with slave laborers of various races and the undead, such as skeletons, ghouls, ghasts, wights, and zombies. At night the polyandrium became a dangerous and scary area, filled with roaming undead awakened from the ancient mass-graves.

The Cult of the Dragon's operations in the Polyandrium was led by Ryngoth and his loyal awakened dire badger Zulkir.

Close proximity to the forest also meant the wild creatures often could be found in the area, such as trolls, giant wasps, giant spiders, and phase spiders.

## Powder of magical detection

Powder of magical detection was a type of magical powder.

Description
This powder was extremely fine and consisted of minute, crystalline granules. They typically came in packets that contained around eleven to twenty pinches of the powder.

Powers
Whenever a mere pinch of this crystalline powder made contact with a magical item, regardless of its size, it would spark and flash with a prismatic array of colors. Users could not discern from the powder the nature or intensity of an item's enchantment, only that the item in question was magical.

Availability

When quaffed this potion allowed individuals to fight as though they were more sturdy and experienced combatants, able to withstand greater harm. Some later iterations of the potion were described as having an effect similar to the spell bless.

Powder of coagulation was a type of magical, yellow powder that helped to heal wounds.

## Potion of sleep

A potion of sleep, also known as potion of somnolence, was a powerful potion that put the imbiber to deep sleep and was extremely dangerous in its undiluted form.

Description
The hazy liquid of the potion was swirled with hypnotic patterns. The concentrated liquid that composed a potion of somnolence created a puff of white mist when the vial was uncorked. It strongly smelled and tasted like blackberries. Drinking the potion made one's mouth tingle as if biting into a mint leaf.

Origins
One of the types of potion of somnolence was distilled out of dark purple mushroom caps, each the size of a dinner plate. These peculiar mushrooms were found in the Feywild all around Darroch Castle, growing in sporadic clusters.

Blood of a werejackal was an ingredient of another recipe for the potion of sleep. It could be a telltale sign as the blood tended to  flake in the potion's liquid. And some were known to use locks of nymph hair as an ingredient.

Powers
Undiluted, potion of somnolence was an incredibly powerful and addictive substance. Just one sip of the highly watered down soporific liquid (one drop of the potion in a vial-full of drinking water) was enough to send the user to sleep in less time than it took to count down from ten. A typical dosage was just one mouthful and it was enough to last several hours.

History
Around 1396 DR, Japheth Donard used a dangerously concentrated potion of somnolence to create a sleeping-aid-like elixir to help Anusha Marhana, a young spell-scarred human noble, to access her unusual power of projecting a ghost-like astral form in times of danger. The ability was only triggered during sleep.

## Podol Plaza

Podol Plaza was the open-air market plaza in the city of Phlan, located on the Podol Street, right by the city gates overlooking the Valhingen Graveyard to the city's north.

Description
Podol Plaza was historically the busiest and biggest marketplace in Phlan. It was strategically constructed to be by the main gates, giving an easy access to travelers and traders, it was close to the warehouses, and the city's docks. The plaza organically became the center for trade. Banking houses of Old Phlan were located around the plaza.

Services
Apart from copious trade from all around Faerun, young kids were known to sell wide sheets of paper on the corners of the plaza with the day's news for 1 cp.

History
Circa 1340 DR, the city was laid in ruins and Podol Plaza was still used as a trading post for orcs, hobgoblins, kobolds, trolls, and goblins, that reigned over the Old Phlan. In 1340 DR, the goblinoids of the ruined Phlan held a big auction to sell a powerful magical item they looted from one of the secret locations of the city. The auction attracted the attention of "the Boss", the possessing spirit Tyranthraxus himself. The evil master of the ruins did not attend the auction however. Instead, he sent his wizard lackey Garwin the Mage and his bodyguard, the ogre Buldwar.

In 1367 DR, the city of Phlan went through an economic boom after the hardships the city suffered from the slew of threats like wars, the coming of Tyranthraxus, and the seizure by Bane. The areas of the city destroyed and damaged by those threats were being rebuilt to serve the citizens and guests of Phlan once again.

## Po

Po was an emperor somewhere in Kara-Tur in the mid–14 century DR.

History
At first, Po's favorite nephew was Hai Chi, who was even considered a candidate to succeed him when he died. After nearly a decade, however, word of Hai Chi's crimes and tyranny—including bashing his wife Shichi No Ken and murdering their child—came to the attention of his father and his uncle the emperor, and they were compelled to bring him to justice. Po sent his soldiers to apprehend Hai Chi and his men as he attacked the Seven Roses Monastery in Pe Nok to kill Shichi and the monks, but they found she had slain him. Emperor Po and Hai Chi's father were even grateful: she had saved them the need for a shameful trial and public execution.

Appendix
Background
Being invented for Encyclopedia Magica Volume IV with mostly unconnected lore, it is unknown where in Kara-Tur and when in Kara-Turan history that Emperor Po reigned. Pe Nok (where Emperor Po apparently has power) is in T'u Lung, supporting that empire. However, none of T'u Lung's known emperors are named "Po". While it's possible Po reigned centuries earlier, perhaps while Pe Nok and T'u Lung were still part of Shou Lung, the use of present tense and provided stats for Shichi imply she is contemporary with other Realmslore, and alive in the 1360s DR. It is possible that Po reigned in one of the other smaller, undetailed nations of Kara-Tur, such as the Warring States, or that Po reigned in T'u Lung after all, succeeding Wai Gada Sinzu (eighth emperor as of 1357 DR) and preceding Wai Yong (tenth emperor by 1375 DR).

References

---

## Potion of heal

Potions of heal were powerful restorative potions that not only cured wounds, but also could remedy harmful conditions such as blindness, deafness, poison, and many more.

Description
Potions of heal were put into small non-transparent flasks that were immediately sealed tight. The liquid itself was uncannily light and a full flask could easily be confused for being empty.

Powers
Potions of heal had a similar effect to the heal spell, it could not only restore the imbiber to full health, but also cure all diseases, most poisons, blindness, deafness, insanity, feeblemindedness, and confusion.

History
Alchemists worked for centuries to master and perfect the recipe for this potion. Eventually, potions of heal entered into circulation sometime in the 14 century, and could be created or purchased throughout the Sword Coast and islands of the Sea of Swords.

## Potion of restoration

Potions of restoration were invaluable magic potions that restored lost life energy, popular among adventurers dealing with powerful undead and clerics of Lathander.

Description

Potions of restoration were usually dispensed in small crystal phials. The liquid itself appeared murky when inside the phial but began to gently glow when brought close to a living creature.

Powers

Potions of restoration came in three power levels, regular, lesser restoration, and greater restoration. When consumed, these potions restored the imbiber's life force and ability drained by the creatures of the negative energy.

Notable Owners
Nazrym - a minor cleric of Bane involved in the Zhent invasion of Shadowdale in the Year of Risen Elfkin, 1375 DR.
Vhazror - an orc champion of Gruumsh in Thar in 1372 DR.

Appendix
See Also
 Restoration flask

## Potion of endurance

Potion of endurance were magic concoctions that increased the drinker's stamina.

Powers
Potion of endurance could be created with a variety of different recipes but all of them had the same effect when consumed. The potion enhanced the imbiber's stamina and overall health but it possessed no healing magic.

History
Potion of endurance were widely available for purchase from traders and wizards of the Sword Coast in the 14 century DR.

Circa 1373 DR, potions of endurance were a part Zhentil Keep garrison's standard equipment and could be found in the satchels of simple guardsmen, cavalry, and skymages. During the same period of time potions of endurance could be found in the wizard stronghold of Telkoun's Tower.

## Pool of Yeven

The Pool of Yeven was a small lake found within the Yevenwood in Battledale.

Geography
The Pool of Yeven was located due east of Lake Sember and south of Cormanthor near the center of the Yevenwood. The lake was fed by the mighty River Ashaba as well as the Semberflow and Glaemril.

A potion of sweet water, also known as simply sweet water, was a liquid solution that could purify any liquid into a drinkable state.

Description
Though not intended for consumption, those who had drank a potion of sweet water noted that it had a refreshing taste.

PowersSweet water would neutralize any poisons within a liquid it was poured into, even drow knockout poison. If poured into a potion, it would ruin its magic. One container of this could transform up to  of polluted, salt, or alkaline water into fresh water. It could also turn up to  of acid into purified water. The effects of this potion were permanent.

Later versions of sweet water were capable of nullifying both poisons and diseases present on any food that would be considered a light meal.

In addition, ingestion of this potion could halt the progression of a curse of forgetfulness.

Creation
The sap of a silverbark was considered to be an essential ingredient for brewing sweet water. Many of the possible ingredients were herbal in nature and could be used as alternatives to the rumored ingredient that was powdered unicorn horn. Overall, the process of creating sweet water took thirty minutes.

When diamond dust was combined with certain substances in a secret process, it could also create a potion of sweet water.

Notable Users
Individuals
 Mugly
 Nasr-uddin
 Tsepo Gart
Races & Sentient Creatures
 Sweet water was one of the many types of restorative potions that nymphs commonly owned.

Usages
 Sweet water was one of the many enchanted liquids known to be used in creating blueshine.
 Sweet water could be used to harm the water-adverse desert trolls.
 This potion was a required ingredient for brewing Halaster's quaff.
 Sweet water could be used to neutralize the lingering musk on items sprayed on by skunks.
 Sweet water acted as a material component for a variety of spells, including wandering symbol, Obar's lesser purification, and Azundel's purification.

Trivia
 When injured, the avatar of the goddess Chauntea would gush forth sweet water rather than blood.
 The spell moonveil was capable of nullifying this potion and rendering its taste foul.
 The water and dew gathered from the glade at the heart of a Moonwell was said to display similar effects to sweet water''.

Appendix
Notes

See Also
 Sweet water, a carbonated beverage that the high-end restaurant The Pleasing Platter was known to serve its patrons in the late 15 century, purchased from their local apothecary Tarmock Felaskur.

## Potion of cat's grace

Potions of cat's grace were common magical concoctions that increased the imbiber's dexterity.

Powers
Potions of cat's grace greatly increased the drinker's dexterity, similarly to the effects of the cat's grace spell.

History
Circa 1369 DR, the ruins of Myth Drannor still held magical potions left behind by the remnants of the city. Potions of cat's grace in delicate crystal decanter were among them. By 1372 DR these potions could be found in wide use in many places across the Sword Coast, such as Neverwinter, Daggerford, and as far as the Nelanther Isles.

Circa 1373 DR, potions of cat's grace were part of a standard equipment for the wizards and agents of Thay.

Notable Owners
Thraka, a half-orc mercenary in Sildëyuir in 1372 DR.
Varzek, a drow cleric of Vhaeraun who operated in Cormanthor circa 1373 DR.

## Pool of radiance

A pool of radiance was a naturally occurring magical phenomenon that manifested as a liquid that resembled water notable for its shimmer, glow, and magical radiance. They were one of the most potent sources of arcane energy in the Realms.

Description
The waters of pools of radiance were a form of raw magic. This was because the Weave of Faerûn, on occasion, amassed its energies in one place and the raw arcane energy always manifested as a glowing, radiant liquid. Sages described the pools as like exposed nerve clusters or lymph nodes of the body, serving the same role for the magical Weave. These pools of radiance could manifest in any place, be it in the middle of a scorching desert, a populated city, or a shaded grove of a secluded forest.

The water from pools of radiance could not be bottled or separated from the main body of water. When attempted, the powers of the water dissipated within less than a minute.

Effects

The phenomenon of the pools had a tumultuous history as it was both a blessing and a curse, carrying potent raw magical energy and its connection to the Weave. The pools had no agenda and no human morality and could potentially be misused to a dangerous degree.

An arcane spellcaster could use a pool of radiance as a source of power, bolstering their own abilities to an incredibly high level. This fact made the pools highly sought after by wizards of all alignments and specializations. These pursuits, however, were incredibly perilous as the raw power of a pool was always much stronger than a wizard's will. Most spellcasters who found one of these magical nodes and attempted to manipulate them failed and faced magical backlashes and side effects. Yet many ambitious adventurers searched for pools to bathe in, drink, or just touch the magical waters trying to gain uncanny powers.

The sheer unpredictability of the pools was their main danger. A living creature that came into direct contact with one could be completely unaffected by the exposed raw power if they lacked arcane skill or the innate ability to access the Weave. But when a creature of arcane talents or abilities touched the waters of a pool, they were almost certainly changed forever. These effects were truly random, with some beneficial and some horrible, and an overwhelming number of them resulted in death. Sages were torn on the mechanics of the pools and their effects. Some theorized that creatures of pure heart could be given abilities to overcome their shortcomings after physically contacting a pool. This theory was backed only by an old story about a young boy from Waterdeep.

Another danger of the pools of radiance was their vulnerability to corruption. Throughout history, the forces of evil and ambition discovered ways of tapping into the arcane energies of the pools and corrupting them via magic rituals or with the power of powerful creatures of the planes. When pools were corrupted, they often harnessed powers of death and destruction. These pools were sometimes referred to as pools of darkness. The corruption was not exclusive to evil beings. Good powers were known to "corrupt" pools too, though these cases were mostly accidental. The pools reflected the natures of creatures that successfully tapped into their powers.

Some of the effects on creatures touching a pool of radiance included:
 Growth of black snake scales all over their body while going blind and gaining extraordinary boons to other senses;
 Instant death, returning to existence as an undead;
 Instant aging;
 Transformation into a gilled aquatic being that could only survive in salt water;
 Change of size;
 Instant death with the creature's life force becoming one with the pool;
 An energy drain spell-like effect;
 Transformation into an invisible form of themselves that only revealed their bones;
 Constant emission of carrion stench;
 Gaining the innate ability to telepathically communicate with dragons but be vulnerable to their fear effect;
 Transformation into an undead being by all rules and definitions but remaining in the same form, shape, and skills as when alive;
And many others. All of the effects gained from the pools were permanent.

Notable Powers

The pools of radiance could be used to create many powerful magical effects. The pool in Myth Drannor was used to create a dome of force around Myth Drannor that was virtually impregnable. The same pool, when it was located in Phlan, could corrupt weaker-minded creatures into worship of Tyranthraxus and was powered by the life energy and bodies of slain creatures. The corruption of that pool was achieved by assembling the arcane figure of power, a hexagon that held ioun stones in its corners. When completed, the figure of power empowered the corrupted pool of radiance to not only enslave minds of orcs, kobolds, ogres, and such, but also humans, elves, and dwarves.

The reawakened pool of darkness in Phlan and other cities of the Mooonsea region allowed their corrupter to transport entire cities.

The pool of darkness underneath the tower of Marcus the Red Wizard was powerful enough that, if fueled with the souls of residents of Phlan, it could transform the corrupter into a demigod.

The pool of radiance in Myth Drannor connected to other active and dormant pools and used them to consume life energies of creatures that surrounded them. The same pool was able to spawn undead out of lifeless husks of creatures it devoured. And lastly, the corrupted pool spread tendrils of itself, known as spawn pools across Myth Drannor, through which it dominated the minds of creatures that resided nearby, including dark nagas, orcs, orogs, and ormyrr.

Notable Locations of Pools
 Hillsfar: One of the cities that had a corrupted pool and was sent to Limbo by Bane. The pool in Hillsfar was later reactivated by the Cult of the Dragon.
 Kranun's Crater: This pool was formed by the debris of a shooting star that fell to Toril on the Moonsea North.
Mulmaster: The city became infested with one of the life-draining spawn pools originating from the Cult of the Dragon-corrupted pool in Myth Drannor.
 Myth Drannor: Within the depths of Castle Cormanthor's sub-levels and Elven Catacombs laid a pool of radiance chamber. The pool was corrupted by the Cult of the Dragon in 1369 DR and later dispersed by the Veiled Ones. Another pool was created by Tyranthraxus in the temple of Labelas Enoreth, later destroyed.
 Phlan: the pool taken over by Tyranthraxus and corrupted into a boiling-hot golden-hued pool of darkness. The pool made numerous returns through the Moonsea region's history.
 Shadowdale: One of the cities where the corrupted pool spread under the control of the Cult of the Dragon.
 Shadowfell: pools of radiance could form outside of the Prime Material plane. One such pool could be found in Evernight's Temple of Filth, where it formed around a powerful artifact harmful to the local undead. The ghouls called it the pool of daylight.
 Thay: The infamous Red Wizard Marcus's tower was built on top of a spacious cavern that housed a powerful pool of darkness. It was later transformed into the pool of twilight.
 Waterdeep: The nearby countryside on the outskirts of the City of Splendors had a small pool of radiance, if the stories were to be believed.

History

In the mid–14 century DR, a pool of radiance was discovered in Phlan, in the caves underneath Valjevo Castle. An ancient being called Tyranthraxus slumbered within its magical waters. He possessed the body of a bronze dragon who'd decided to take a bath in the ethereal glowing waters. The parasitic creature used dark arcane rituals to corrupt the pool, turning it into the pool of darkness. Tyranthraxus used the pool's power to take over Valjevo Castle and rule over the monsters who resided in the ruined part of Phlan. A group of heroes including Ren o' the Blade, Shal Bal, and Tarl Desanea rose against the possessing spirit and broke his control of the pool. As Tyranthraxus lost his body, and the pool was defeated, the spirit leaped into the receding waters of raw magical energy, allowing him to escape.

Tyranthraxus possessed the pool, transporting part of it to the ruins of Myth Drannor. The possessing spirit took residence in the ruined temple of Labelas Enoreth and created the new pool of radiance on the second floor of the temple in the form of a majestic fountain. The possessing spirit forged an alliance with powerful beings and organizations and used their knowledge to tap into the power of the pool once again. Tyranthraxus used the pool to place a magical dome of force around the ruins of Myth Drannor, claiming it as his domain. The heroes of Phlan, aided by hero Alias and her companions, eventually defeated Tyranthraxus and used the Gauntlets of Moander to "devour" the corrupted pool.

A decade later, the god Bane assembled an alliance of dark powers, helmed by a Red Wizard of Thay named Marcus to reawaken the pools of radiance in Phlan and other cities across the Moonsea region. He used the pools' power to corrupt them and power his divine magics, transporting the cities into the nether realms. Phlan was sent to a gargantuan underground cavern while other affected cities were swept away to the plane of Limbo. The Heroes of Phlan were within the city when it was abducted, and with the help of new allies, Evaine, Gamaliel, and Miltiades, the heroes prevailed, returning the cities and draining the pools once again.

Following the Time of Troubles in 1358 DR, the pool in Phlan was nothing but ordinary fresh water. Nevertheless, the city placed guards around the pool chamber, as well as in front of Kuto's Well, its entrance.

In the, the Cult of the Dragon seized a newly formed pool of radiance in the caverns underneath Castle Cormanthor. Led by the powerful sorceress and Wearer of the Purple Kya Mordrayn, and advised by her general, the dracolich Pelendralaar, the cultists used their dark arcane knowledge to corrupt the pool, turning its silvery clear waters sickeningly green. Through the corrupted pool, Kya was able to reach other active and dormant pools across Faerûn, corrupting them and, in turn, draining magic and life forces across the Moonsea region. Mordrayn was able to tap into Myth Drannor's mythal using the pools to turn it into her weapon. The ritual required the dracolich to be completely submerged in the pool, allowing the cultist full control of raw corrupted magic. The Cult of the Dragon was able to use corrupted pools to turn creatures fallen to the life-stealing magics of the pools into undead, relentlessly assaulting the same cities that were taken by Bane years ago.

Two groups of heroes stopped the Cult of the Dragon's plans: the Veiled Ones sent by Elminster Aumar and accidental heroes who were searching for villagers abducted by the cult. Working independently, both groups stopped the cult by destroying the dracolich's phylactery and the undead wyrm. Kya Mordrayn was dragged into the Abyss by her dark benefactors for failing in her mission. And the Veiled Ones repaired the mythal and used the Gauntlets of Moander to "devour" the corrupted pool.

Circa the, a pool formed in the Shadowfell's twisted echo of Neverwinter, Evernight. The local ghoul population was willing to tell anyone who listened about the pool of daylight, bright as the sun, radiating energy harmful to the undead, burning any such creature that entered its chamber. A mysterious magical device at the bottom of the pool affected the raw magic, turned it into a weapon of sorts. The ghoul of the Temple of Filth guarded the pool mostly to keep it from falling into some do-gooder's hands and being used against them. It was unclear if the pool of radiance formed around the item or if someone threw it into the pool of raw magic.

In the, during the Cult of the Dragon's seizure of power in the Moonsea region, the Red Wizard Rorreth Monforoth allied himself with the cult and was set on a quest to find the inert pool of radiance in Phlan and reconnect it with the Weave. The cult was planning to corrupt the pool of radiance and make it supply them with an army of the dead.

In the, a meteor formed Kraunun's Crater and spawned a pool of radiance at its bottom. One of the stone giants of the area touched the magical waters and was possessed by Tyranthraxus, who at some point in the past had retreated into the pools. The possessor took over the green dragon Vorgansharax of the Cult of the Dragon as the cultists arrived to claim the powerful raw magics of the pool. The Cult was able to connect the newly formed pool to the old waning pool in Phlan.

Deactivation

There were very few methods of closing, dispersing, or destroying a pool of radiance. If left uncorrupted, a pool of radiance eventually disappeared back into the Weave. Some rare and powerful artifacts could affect the pools, usually items that carried divine magic. Using spells akin to dispel magic rendered a pool inert for one minute only.
 Gauntlets of Moander: The unique set of enchanted gauntlets that were created to destroy and "devour" pools of radiance. The gauntlets were inherently tied to the pools to such a level that when there were no active pools of radiance, the gauntlets were dormant, losing even the most basic of enchantments.
 Hammer of Tyr: The holy relic of the god of justice Tyr was instrumental in countering the raw power of the pool of darkness corrupted by Bane. The relic needed to be simply submerged into the waters of the pool with a prayer to the deity.

Certain enchanted items could also detect the pools of radiance. For example, the Helm of Dragons could sense them from as far as .

Mythals, notably the one that was placed around the ruins of Myth Drannor, were known to protect against and stop spreading of the corruption of pools of darkness via its spawn pools.

Rumors & Legends
 The legendary status of the pools of radiance came not only from historical accords but also from countless stories and legends of creatures encountering the "magical pools of water" and gaining remarkable powers and abilities. The sages confirmed some of these stories as being truthful, while others were greatly exaggerated over time.
 A popular tale spoke of a Waterdhavian child who was blinded by a sickness early in life. One day he came across a small pool of magical, radiant water while playing outside. The pool had not been there before and disappeared soon after. Parched, the blind boy drank from the pool. Minutes later, he was clutching his eyes, screaming of the bright light hurting his eyes. As the evening fell, the boy realized he could see in total darkness as if it were daylight.
 Many old stories that talked about the pools also mentioned the ancient creature of possession, somehow intricately tied to the magics of the pools. Tyranthraxus was one of the very few creatures who could control, move, and even reside within the pools of radiance.

Appendix

}}Potions of treasure finding were a type of potion that aided their imbibers in discovering treasure.

Powers
When consumed, these potions provided their drinker with a sort of "directional sense" in regards to treasure, enabling them to feel or sense the general direction of the nearest mass, but not its exact location. The only substances this sense could not penetrate through were special magical wards and walls lined with lead. This mass had to be within  to be detected and its overall weight needed to be equal to at least 10,000 copper pieces or 100 gems.

What these potions classified as "treasure" was solely gems, jewelry, and metals considered valuable, such as copper, silver, electrum, gold, and platinum. Those considered "worthless" would not be detected by these potions. Magical items were also not considered valuable by a potion of treasure finding unless their body consisted in part of some form of valuable gem or metal.

VariantsPaste of treasure finding: The people of Serôs had a thick, gelatinous paste version of this potion, suitable for use in their underwater environment. Like other pastes of Serôs, a paste of treasure finding consisted of kelp and other viscous substances. It was stored in either clamshells or flexible tubes made from eelskin.

History
In the mid–14 century DR, a traveling mercantile store in the Mooney And Sons Circus known as Glitz's And Klax's Potions And Philters would sell potions that had one-tenth their normal power. This included potions of treasure finding''.

Creation
Suggested special ingredients for such potions were six different gemstones crushed into a powder, the scale of a gold dragon, or the dust of a diamond.

## Potion of delusion

Potions of delusion were potions that caused those who drank them to believe they had imbibed and gained the effects of any number of other magical potions.

History
In 1358 DR, one of the apprentice magic-users in Zhentil Keep failed in creating a potion and it resulted in a potion of delusion.

In the 14 century DR, whenever the Wizards Guild of Ravens Bluff was coerced into surrendering magical potions or gravely suspected that an adventurer requesting them would put them to misuse, they would instead gift potions of delusion or faulty potions of levitation.

Creation
Suggested special ingredients for such potions were the flesh of a doppelganger, the "ichor" or spiritual essence of a rakshasa, and in the case of Zakhara the blood of an al-jahar.

It was also said that there was a small chance that any failed attempt in brewing a potion would result in a potion of delusion.

Notable Owners
 Multiple potions of delusion were owned by the balor Ter-soth.
 Multiple potions of delusion could be found in the treasure horde of Tiamat's Lair.

Appendix
Appearances

References

---

## Potion of dragon's blood

Potions of dragon's blood were potions imbued with divine magic, used by the clerics of Kelemvor to consecrate shrines.

History
Following the Time of Troubles and the apotheosis of the new god of death Kelemvor, many of his new followers set out on a holy mission of claiming shrines and temple of then perished god of death, Myrkul. Part of the consecration ceremony when claiming Myrkul's shrines was using the potions of dragon's blood to cleanse the corruption. The consecration ceremony also broke any enchantments that were placed upon the shrine.

In 1370 DR, a Kelemvorite wandering priest Daland descended to Undermountain on the mission to consecrate the shrines in the temple of Myrkul on the Dungeon Level. While in the temple's crypts, Daland picked up a cursed scroll and became trapped like a living statue. Luckily, soon after, an agent of Khelben Arunsun arrived at the temple in search of the Black Wand of Myrkul. Daland begged for help. He asked the adventurer to use his potion of dragon's blood consecrating the shrine and dispelling the curse.

## Pockall

Pockall was a Netherese arcanist and spell inventor who lived during the Golden Age.

History
Pockall invented four spells during their life. The first three spells involved invisibility, and they were: improved invisibility, which came in, invisibility, which came in, and invisibility, 10' radius, which was invented in . Pockall's final spell was rather different from their previous ones. This spell, charm monster, was developed in .

Polybeus was a Netherese arcanist who lived during the Age of Discovery.

History
This arcanist developed a single spell during their lifetime. This spell, continual light, was developed in .

## Powder of good taste

Powder of good taste was a type of magical powder used for culinary purposes.

Description
This powder had a white hue and it was packaged in packets.

Powers
Placing a single pinch of this powder on any  of food, no matter its condition, would cause it to taste delicious. Though the appearance of the food would not be altered.

Notable Owners
 Solimar, a chef and wizard at the Ravens Bluff restaurant The Painted Boat, owned some powder of good taste.

Appendix

## Potion of elemental control

Potions of elemental control were potions that allowed an imbiber to influence elementals.

Powers
Those who consumed one of these potions could influence one or two elementals within  of them in a manner similar to charm monster. The chance of this working was greater when just focusing on one elemental. Elementals controlled by a wizard also had a greater chance of resisting the potion's effect.

In addition, those who consumed a potion of elemental control had a 50% chance of dispelling an elemental summoned by the spell conjure elemental.

Creation
One of the known ingredients for these oils was some material from the appropriate elemental plane.

Notable Users
 These were among the magical items commonly used by planewalkers and could be purchased in the markets of Sigil at prices lower than those on the Prime Material plane.
 Some sha'irs were known to craft these potions.
 House Baenre kept a potion of elemental control geared specifically towards elemental earth in case they ever had trouble with derros.

Appendix
References

---

## Poppy

Poppies were a type of flowering plant that produced an intoxicating or blissful effect when smoldered.

Habitats
Places in the Realms where poppies could be found growing included the following:
 In the Dalelands, poppies could be found growing in Archendale and Tasseldale.
 In the Hordelands, blue poppies could be found growing in the Katakoro Plateau. Poppies could also rarely be found in the Raurin and Quoya Desert.
 Poppies could be found in parts of Kara-Tur, such as a large field of them known as the Golden Bowl and in parts of the Malatran Plateau.
 Poppies were grown decoratively in the town of Auckney near the Spine of the World and in the Moonsea region.

Usage
 On the Malatran Plateau, poppy flowers were used by some Nubari to create a yellow-orange dye.
Culinary
 Poppy seeds were commonly used as a seasoning.  Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold these for around 8 gold pieces per .
 In the Greenfields region of the Western Heartlands, some served bagels with sprinklings of poppy seeds.
 Poppyseed cakes were a baked food found in Hlondeth, the City of Serpents in the Vilhon Reach.
Magic
 Some druids found poppy seeds to be useful in their magic.
 If ground into a powder, poppy seeds could be used as a material component for casting the reverse of the spell true seeing.
 An alchemical suspension of poppy flowers was the main ingredient in brewing potions of mind reading.

Trivia
 Poppy seed oil was poisonous to werebadgers.
 Red poppies were a favored plant of the goddesses Loviatar and Sune.

Appendix
Appearances

References

---

## Potion of animal speaking

Potions of animal speaking were magical concoctions that allowed their imbibers to verbally speak with animals for a period of time.

Description
Potions of animal speaking smelled of hay, musk, and manure. The effects of this potion lasted for one day.

Creation
A potion of animal speaking could me created by mixing an essence of acorn truffles and certain types of alchemical salts.

Notable Users
 Arron, a hin druid merchant from the Emerald Grove in the late 15 century DR.

 Derryth Bonecloak, a dwarven potionmaker and merchant from the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.
 Volothamp Geddarm, a famous, or infamous writer, carried potions of animal speaking during his expedition into the Chionthar Valley and investigation of the Cult of the Absolute in the late 15 century DR.

## Portia Dzuth

Portia Dzuth was an adventurer in Undermountain in the early 1490s DR.

History
In 1492 DR, Portia and her party were hired by Lady Wylynd Moonstar of Waterdeep to find Umbraxakar the shadow dragon and transform him back into Glyster. However, she and her companions were captured by the Dark Army of the Night and Portia was enthralled by Keresta Delvingstone. Afterward Keresta used her as human shield and source of food for her vampire spawns.

Personality
Portia started to suffer the despair of the Shadowfell, believing she'd been imprisoned inside Vanrakdoom for a very long time. She thought that to redeem Umraxakar it was necessary to rid the dragon of despair but had no idea how.

Relationship
Under her charm spell, Portia believed Keresta to be a trusted friend.

## Pork pie

Pork pie was a dish of pork meat baked in a flaky pastry pie known in Faerûn as of the mid–14 century DR.

Availability
 The Inn of the Dripping Dagger, an establishment in Waterdeep, offered patrons pork pies that were either mild or hotly spiced at a cost of one copper piece. For those traveling, they also offered them cold or wrapped in ice and leaves.
 Hriiat Fine Pastries in Waterdeep was known for savory "bite-pies", including one filled with pork.
 The Sleeping Cat restaurant in Wheloon served pork pastries in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.
 The Rising Moon inn in Highmoon was known for its filling pork pies in the mid-to-late 14 century DR, courtesy of chef and Harper agent, Rhiia Duskmantle.
 Rose's Tea Room in Raven's Bluff served hot, spicy pork pies to the crowds from the city's Coliseum.

History
As of 1358 DR, the sage Warthendel owned a magic skillet that could produce a pork pie (or an omelet or fried fish) once every 30 minutes.

Mayor Tobald tucked into a huge pork pie at the Bold Bard in Ghars, Cormyr, in Eleint of 1367 DR. It looked so good, Jasper ordered one himself.

## Poisoned Quill

The Poisoned Quill were a tribe of merfolk in the Sea of Fallen Stars in the 15 century DR.

Activities
This tribe was particularly known for hunting.

Possessions
All members of this tribe wielded spears that were longer than the average length. They also carried small pouches that contained the spines of pufferfish.

History
In the late-15 century DR, the sea elves of Myth Nantar sent a group of adventurers to try and convince nearby merfolk tribes, including the Poisoned Quill, to aid them in a coming assault from the Red Wizards of Thay. The then leader of the tribe, Asirae, was eager to join the fray and felt her people were prepared for it. Though she was concerned that the other tribes, the Cerulean Shores and Deep Coves, were not as well trained enough for such a battle.

Members
 Asirae Shipwrecker, the leader of the tribe in the late-15 century DR.

## Potion of toughening

Potions of toughening were potions that possessed healing and fortifying effects, common in Waterdeep circa 1370 DR.

Powers
When consumed, a potion of toughening temporarily increased the imbiber's fortitude, health, and patched bodily harm suffered. Unlike regular potion of healing, this concoction's effects dispersed after 70 to 120 minutes. If the imbiber suffered significant damage before consuming the potion and not healed by the time the magic expired, the imbiber returned to the vulnerable state or even became unconscious. If the drinker was knocked out after the potion of toughening expired, they continued to suffer damage and could eventually perish if not healed in time.

Another danger of consuming a potion of toughening came from drinking at the second portion before the effect of the first one expired. The second dose had no restorative effect whatsoever but caused the drinker to suffer from a system shock and possibly die from it immediately.

History
In 1370 DR, potions of toughening were popular among the daring Undermountain delvers and could be found among loot taken from unfortunate deceased adventurers by the inhabitants of the infamous Waterdeep dungeon.

## Powder horn

Powder horns were a type of small portable vessel used to store and transport either gunpowder or smokepowder.

Description

Powder horns typically took the form of a shoulder-strung horn or leather flask, typically waterproof. They could hold up to  of either powder. As a firearm typically required  gunpowder or smokepowder to be reloaded, this meant that a powder horn held enough for most firearms to take 32 shots.

Notable Users
Races & Sentient Creatures
 Rock gnomes could often be found carrying powder horns for use with pistols.
Regions & Settlements
 The people of Lantan often had powder horns for use with their firearms.
 Powder horns could sometimes be found in use by those with firearms in the Nelanther Isles.

Trivia
Gunpowder and smokepowder were typically sold within either powder horns or powderkegs.

## Potion of clarity

Potions of clarity were magic potions that cleared and fortified the imbiber's mind.

Description
Potions of clarity brewing process included pouring the piping-hot concoction over scorched, pungent beans. The resulting potion had a strong smell and did not appear clear, contrary to its name.

Powers
These foul-smelling potions immediately after consumption cleared the drinker's mind, flooding them with overwhelming feelings of calm and purpose. That feeling persisted as long at the potions' effects remained active, fortifying the mind's defenses against mind-affecting magic spells and monster abilities, similar to that of the clarity spell.

History
Circa Year of the Banner, 1368 DR, a myconid potion brewer, the Concoctor, was forced to serve the eccentric drow Baeloth the Entertainer of the Black Pits in the Underdark. Potions of clarity were among concoctions the fungus-man was selling to the Black Pits' gladiators.

The same year, these potions were available for purchase from the Sorcerous Sundries of Baldur's Gate, the Temple of Helm, Roger the Fence, of Athkatla, Amn, Wallace of Trademeet, and the bazars of Ust Natha.

In the late 14 century DR, potions of clarity were wide-spread throughout the Sword Coast and the Moonsea regions.

## Pohta

Pohtas were simple portable living structures built by the Numtanajd people of the High Desert in west Zakhara.

Description
A pohta was a temporary home constructed by stretching wool blankets over a rectangle frame of sticks. A pohta could be tied into a bundle for easy transport. Woolen mats were placed on the floor to shield against the hot sand of the High Desert. Pohtas were often arranged in tight circles so herd animals could be penned on the inside for added protection against predators.

## Porto Churlgo

Porto Churlgo was an ambitious and backstabbing son of Millie Churlgo who produced the infamous Churlgo Cheese in the Eastern Farmlands of Daggerford circa 1372 DR.

Description
This young halfling was a lazy individual. His chores and farm work were always done slowly as if he always expected others to pick up slack. Porto dated farm life and dreamed about moving to a big city to paramour lovely city lasses and gamble to his heart's content.

Porto's attitude was inflammatory and he was rude to any passersby assuming they were there only to gawk and make fun of the farmers. Porto often put up false bravado, however his words and deeds were often at odds.

History
Porto, along with other Churlgos moved their farm from the Dalelands to Daggerford following a nasty extortion business with the Iron Throne. In 1372 DR, the farm where they resided was still noticeably un-lived-in, but the cheese-making business was in full swing.

Eventually, Porto Churlgo grew disgruntled with his mother, how she ran the business and the only thing he wanted to do is trike rich and move to Waterdeep to gamble, drink, and wench. He often tried to convince Millie Churlgo that raising cattle and growing grain was more profitable than cheese-making, but the halfling matriarch was committed to the craft that Churlgos polished over several generations. His farm work included taking care of the farm's cows and making deliveries of Churlgo Cheeses to the neighboring Gillian's Hill from where the cheese was sent tout throughout the Sword Coast.

Harboring a deep resentment for his life and the farm, Porto started secretly working for the agent of the Iron Throne, Yentai, who followed the halflings from the Dalelands. With Porto's help, Yentai organized raids on the cheese caravans trying to run Millie Churlgo out of business, while selling the stolen cheese as his own.

Porto's betrayal came to light when Millie asked the Hero of Daggerford to accompany one of the cheese shipments to the distributor. The cart was ambushed by a band of brigands who were surprised to see Porto with a well-arm guard. When the scuffle was over, the Hero found instructions for the bandits signed by Yentai. As the Iron Throne's plan started unraveling, Yentai gave Millie an ultimatum. Surrender the recipe and leave the business forever. However, the Hero had proof that Porto conspired behind his mother's back. Greatly saddened, Millie Churlgo ordered her son to leave the farm forever.

## Pog

Pog was a rock gnome guard of Gnomengarde in the late 15 century DR.

Activities
Alongside Ulla, Pog was one of Gnomengarde's sentries. It was his job to hail any outsiders in Common, and to attack anyone he deemed to be a threat.

Abilities
In addition to his innate gnomish abilities, Pog (like everyone else in Gnomengarde) had some training as a wizard. In combat, he preferred to attack with spells like ray of frost and magic missile.

History
When Gnomengarde came under attack by a mimic in the late 15 century DR, the only information given to the gnomes by their king, Korboz, was to attack any shapechangers on sight. Unfortunately, this left Pog and his partner Ulla in a predicament because anyone could be a shapechanger. They were thus highly suspicious of any outsiders, and were unlikely to let anyone pass without a fight unless they could persuade the sentries otherwise or unless they had an audience with King Korboz or King Gnerkli.

## Pomegranate

Pomegranates, or, araed, as they were known in Faerûn, were fruit-bearing trees, commonly grown in the wild and sometimes cultivated in the lands south of the Sword Coast.

Availability
Pomegranates were occasionally imported from the southern regions (excluding Shar) to the Eastern and Western Heartlands as well as Sword Coast North. These shipments were often merely an afterthought, when trade ships had a bit of extra space. Pomegranates were considered cheap fruit as it grew almost everywhere in the south in the wild.

In the City of Splendors, Waterdeep, Khelben Arunsun had an ensorcelled kitchen cupboard within the Blackstaff Tower that, when opened, brought forth an overbearing pomegranate tree, especially favored by Danilo Thann in his childhood. Lady Laeral Silverhand also liked to imbibe a glass or two of refreshing pomegranate nectar.

Pomegranates were consumed raw by the inhabitants of Anauroch.

In Zakhara, pomegranate, coconut palms, banana, orange, lemon, plum, and fig trees were cultivated and often housed ashira, benevolent tree spirits. Notably, Mosque of a Thousand Hummingbirds in Dihliz was filled with blooming and pomegranate-bearing trees. The Pearl Cities, wealthy Zakharan port cities, cultivated many legumes, fruits, and vegetables, inclusion pomegranates.

Outside of the Prime Material plane, pomegranates could be found in the Feywild. Archfey Neifion served these crimson fruits in his Darroch Castle in 1396 DR.

Usage
In the southern regions, pomegranates got more use in making sauces or dyes, rather than eating them raw.
In Calimshan, pomegranate and grape juice was a popular libation.
Some variations of mummification process included several preserving and aerobatic plants and substances, such as cinnamon, ginger, rose, myrrh, frankincense, and pomegranate.
In the land of Thay, pomegranates were candied and served as sweet treats, loved even by cruel Zulkirs of the Red Wizards.
Pomegranate seeds were known to be used by wizards as a material component of certain spells.
Pomegranates possessed some medicinal properties, namely, it was used as a nerve sedative, and a wormer agent.

Significance
There was a fable about a mortal woman who was granted passage through the lower planes a long time ago. The woman was given one single rule, not to eat anything during her journey through the path ahead. She followed them apart from one little indiscretion. She ate three pomegranate seeds. When the time came to leave the planes, a wall of invisible force trapped her and no-one has heard from the lady ever since.

## Pool of Tefnut

The Pool of Tefnut was an oasis that would appear and disappear in Raurin. It was named for the goddess Tefnut, one of the members of the Pharaonic pantheon who was forced to stay behind instead of traveling to Realmspace to become part of the Mulhorandi pantheon.

Overview
The pool of Tefnut would remain in its location until a good-aligned person looked into its waters. They would see two almond-shaped eyes, which would volunteer to answer their questions. Once they had asked three questions, the oasis would vanish. All travelers who had witnessed the oasis would find their water reserves refilled, and their thirst quenched.

## Potion of glibness

Potions of glibness, also known as philters of glibness, was a potion that mimicked the effects of the glibness spell.

Powers
Those who imbibed a potion of glibness had their speech altered to sound smooth and convincing, no matter what lie they told. When put under magical scrutiny, such as by the spell detect lie, they will at the might be perceived as somewhat misrepresenting information. If put under a magical effect would force them to speak truthfully, such as zone of truth, an imbiber had a chance to resist. The chance of fibbing being detected was even less for charlatan bards that imbibed.

Notable Users
 Potions of glibness counted among the potions that Master Harpers were proficient in creating.
 The Child of Silver in Huzuz.
 Hamid al-Bashshar, a member of the Corsair Council in Hawa, carried several a philters of glibness. T
 Jamal al-Yindannim, a member of the Brotherhood of the True Flame.
 Jarda, a halfling sa'luk inhabiting Huzuz in 1367 DR.
 Jeric, a demarche.
 Jhenna Iliathor.
 Muli, a Zakharan conman and sa'luk.
 Netocris bin Shalah, a yuan-ti pureblood ambassador to the necropolis of Ysawis in 1367 DR.
 Shalush Myrkeer.
 Vexia, an annis hag that lived in Cormanthyr in the 14 century DR.

History
Sometime around 1367 DR, an Al-Badia student at the College of Religion and Science in Qadib by the name of Aziz al Kajam had squandered his study opportunities and abysmally in his courses to the point that he was kicked out. Worried of what his father and tribe would think of him, the young man panicked and before leaving the city stole a philter of glibness from the college laboratory, one of many minor potions that he had been studying in his course on basic magic. When he returned to his tribe's home in the Furrowed Mountains he imbibed this philter of glibness, using its powers to spin an elaborate lie of his natural philosophy teacher Hakim ibn Rashad having it out for him because of him being an Al-Badian. The philter of glibness was so effective that it managed to fool both his father, Diyab al Kaban, and his tribe's hakima.

## Potion of polymorph self

A potion of polymorph self was an enchanted potion that replicated the effects of the polymorph self spell.

Description
Potions of polymorph self were bottled in small glass vials and shimmered with multiple scintillating colors of the magical liquid.

Powers
The imbiber of this potion could change their form into that of any corporeal creature. The rough size range of possible creatures was as small as a bird to as large as a hippopotamus. The imbiber gained the form of locomotion and breathing of the selected creature, but not any other natural, special or magical abilities. Any equipment worn by the imbiber was melded into their new form. The imbiber's intelligence remained the same so spellcasting was still viable assuming the new form allowed for it.

Varities
Lizard Marsh Potions of polymorph self from the Lizard Marsh allowed the imbiber to transform into a giant spider, troll, umber hulk, pixie, or a zombie.
Paste of polymorph self The people of Serôs had a thick, gelatinous paste version of this potion, suitable for use in their underwater environment. Like other pastes of Serôs, a paste of polymorph consisted of kelp and other viscous substances. It was stored in either clamshells or flexible tubes made from eelskin.

Notable Owners
Durissa Thornion, an illusionist from Chondath, carried a potion of polymorph self.
The Green Hag of Lizard Marsh possessed a rich collection of magic potions, tinctures, and brews. Among them were potion of polymorph of her own creation.

Powers

Potions of mirrored eyes could be purchased from the innkeeper of the Ulgoth's Beard Inn, a small hamlet of the same name in the vicinity of Baldur's Gate.

## Poison touch

Poison touch was a divine alteration and necromancy spell used by priests devoted to Talona, enabling them to inflict corrosive harm with a touch.

Effects
This spell created a brown radiance around the caster's hand. The caster needed to touch a target within five seconds or the spell would be wasted. The caster could touch a target anywhere on their body regardless of what they were wearing.

The corrosion would burn a hole through metal armor and cause ghastly welts to appear on the target's skin. Furthermore, the poison caused the victim to become sluggish with all of their body movements and sometimes caused the victim to pass into unconsciousness.

Components
This spell required only verbal and somatic components to cast.

## Portyr

Portyr ( was a wealthy and influential patriar family of Baldur's Gate.

Activities
The Portyrs made their coin in maritime shipping, investing, and trading.

Base of Operations
The Portyr estate was an unassuming manor in the city's Temples District.

History
After Grand Duke Valarken and the Band of the Red Moon's failed coup in the mid-15 century DR, Dillard Portyr, the only duke to survive, was named Grand Duke of Baldur's Gate. He reformed the Parliament of Peers, granting the institution more power over the day-to-day business of running the city. As Grand Duke, Dillard Portyr was known for being easily manipulated and conflict-averse. He ceded the title of Grand Duke to Ulder Ravengard in the late 15 century.

 Dillard Portyr, Duke of Baldur's Gate. He survived two wives and three sons.
 Liara Portyr, a blaze in the Flaming Fist and the commander of Fort Beluarian in Chult.

These potions were often bottled in glass decanters, decorated with filigree.

Powers
Consumption of a potion of true seeing was similar to a powerful wizard casting the spell of true seeing on the imbiber. While the potion's effects were active, the drinker could freely see in normal and magical darkness, see through illusions and invisibility, not be deterred by mirror image spell, and could see things and creatures displaced from the Prime Material plane to the Ethereal plane.

History
Circa 1369 DR, several of these potions could be recovered from the ruins of grand city of Myth Drannor. These potions were used by the Cult of the Dragon who occupied the ruins in 1369 DR, as well as their drow allies from the noble House Freth.

## Polymorph other

Polymorph other was a powerful and common spell that let one change another individual into another form.

Effects
When cast, the targeted creature was completely transformed. Not only was their physical appearance altered, but their abilities, intelligence, personality and skills as well. The targeted creature could only be transformed into another creature, the caster was unable to use this spell to transform an individual into an object or a plant nor a non-corporeal form. The transformation was a shock on the target's system, there was a chance the targeted creature was killed by the spell. If killed while the spell was in effect, the targeted creature reverted to their natural form. Any equipment in the physical possession of a targeted creature was melded into their new form.

Once transformed, the targeted creature acquired not only the physical form but also the abilities of their new body. There were some limits. If transformed into a form whose natural abilities included innate magical abilities, the polymorphed creature did not inherit these abilities. If a doppelganger, druid, lycanthrope or other natural shapeshifters were targeted with this spell, the effect of the spell was limited and their form was transformed for only a very short period of time.

If the polymorphed individual lost their original mentality and personality and fully adapted the mentality of their new form, they effectively became their new form, even acquiring any innate magical abilities. The only way to reverse this was if a wish spell was cast upon them. If the polymorphed individual only lost their individuality to their new form, a dispel magic spell would revert their physical form to their original form but they still retained the individuality of their polymorphed form. For example, if a human who was polymorphed into a copper dragon came to believe fully they were indeed a copper dragon, after returning to their human form, they now believed they were a copper dragon who has been polymorphed into a human.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component, the cocoon of a caterpillar. The spell cast by wu jen used the wings of a moth wrapped in a piece of silk.

History
This spell was invented in  by Netherese arcanist Quantoul. It was the last spell he developed and was originally called Quantoul's othermorph. The spell was found in several spellbooks such as the Book of Bats, Bowgentle's Book and the Tome of the Unicorn.

## Potion of dragonbreath

Potions of dragonbreath were magical concoctions that could simulate the infamous and dangerous fiery breath of a dragon. In the post-Spellplague era these potions came in a wider variety, simulating the range of breaths characteristic of chromatic dragons.

Description
These small red potions were hot and fiery in appearance and taste.

Powers
When consumed, the magics of these potions allowed the drinker to produce a huge gout of hot burning flames out of their mouth. The flames were similar to those of an actual dragon and similar to the effects of the dragon's breath spell.

In the post-Spellplague era, in addition to huge gouts of hot burning flames an imbiber could breath a gout of searing acid, freezing cold, poisonous gas, or streaks of lightning.

History
Circa 1372 DR, the Green Hag of Lizard Marsh, who feuded with a lizardman shaman Uzzak-Szalzik, possessed a rich collection of magic potions, tinctures, and brews. Among them were potions of dragonbreath of her own creation. These potions were available to any of the green hag's allies against her sworn lizardfolk enemy.
