## Potion of dracolich transformation

Potions of dracolich transformation were magical potions consumed by dragons as a part of the process of becoming undead dracoliches, created by the Cult of the Dragon.

Description
Potions of dracolich transformation were an integral part of the process to transform a living wyrm into a dracolich. The exact composition of each position varied and was prepared for each individual dragon separately using a wide selection of ingredients, depending on the age and type of the dragon. All variants, however, were brewed using seven arcane ingredients that included vampire blood, a potion of evil dragon control, a potion of invulnerability, and the ichor of a ghost.

Powers
The potion was imbibed after the future dracolich's phylactery prepared. This concoction was poisonous to any other creatures but dragons. The lethality of the potion was severe, making survival extremely unlikely.

Creation
In order to prepare a serving of this potion, a spellcaster needed to assemble all the required ingredients and use the animate dead spell.

History
This potion was used in turning Pelendralaar into his undead form sometime before 1369 DR. During the Cult of the Dragon's occupation of the ruins of Myth Drannor, Kya Mordrayn's lieutenants prepared another serving of the potion, presumably to transform Pelendralaar's daughter Palasiraks at a later date.

## Pokarr Gompa

Pokarr Gompa was a monastery (a gompa in the Tabotan language) of the Way of Enlightenment in Tabot. It was nicknamed the Right Hand of U'Chan.

Geography
It stood in central Tabot, at the northern edge of the Haraki Ridge. Its position south of the capital U'Chan Gompa gave it its title; it stood opposite Lii Gompa, the Left Hand of U'Chan.

Description
The monastery was formerly a fortress, built deep into the side of a mountain. The old keep still stood at the heart of the gompa. Beneath it lay tunnels or catacombs that stretched for miles.

Defenses
The entrance to Pokarr Gompa had three wide gates made of stone that could be swung open or closed in a moment. For defense of the realm, it boasted a strong militia, including a fearsome cavalry, and they were known to have beat back attackers on countless occasions. Temple guards were garbed in dark-green pants and blouses, and wielded scimitars.

Interior
Pokarr Gompa famously housed many of great treasures and tomes of Tabot. In its four libraries were such works as Wang Tcu Lor's religious treatises, Soo Enlahm's journals and natural histories, and Ischan's and Doka'zar's masterpieces. The three separate treasure rooms kept secure the religious relics of Tabot, including holy remains, bones made of stone, a magical tapestry that hypnotized those who viewed it, four of the nine golden eggs of Ghastar, and many more. Also in the treasure rooms, rather than the libraries, was a five-volume holy book written by the first High Lord of Oceans, H'Dang Li.

History
It was founded some time between  and, when fifteen groups of monks and temple guardians escaped persecution in Shou Lung and journeyed southwest in search of a holy land that would remain pure and lasting, ideal for isolated hermitages. One of these groups built the beginnings of Pokarr.

The warrior Zife Yur went into the catacombs below Pokarr Gompa in  and was never seen again. Thereafter, the lamas forbade anyone from going into them.

For a great festival at the Temple of Heaven's Heart on Mount E'kwong circa 2607, Abbot Mo Kin chose the monk scribe Pang to represent Pokarr Gompa, releasing him from an eight-year-long vow of silence in the process. On a pilgrimage to the site, accompanied by five temple guards, Pang spoke of Tabot to the Dragon Lord Mei Lung, who was attending as a guest from Shou Lung.

Relationships
Pokarr Gompa was the primary rival to neighboring Lii Gompa. This might be why Pang spoke little of Lii to Mei Lung. Pang was also dismissive of Nafeen Gompa and Zotung Gompa, declaring they did not follow "the true and holy laws of the High Lord" as Pokarr did.

Pokarr Gompa regularly sent delegations to Do'dzin Gompa for four of its annual festivals.

Inhabitants
It housed over a thousand lamas and novices. They trained with the sword, learnt calligraphy, and were permitted to practice one other artform, as a devotion to the deities. They commonly chose to make pottery, using clay from south of Rokstang, and their works were renowned throughout Tabot. The greatest of their potters worked magic into the clay.

Pokarr Gompa was led by Abbot Mo Kin around .

Activities
The abbot of Pokarr was also commander of the militia, and was responsible for patrolling Tabot's eastern border with Shou Lung north of Mount E'kwong.

## Power word, maladroit

Power word, maladroit was an enchantment spell that made the target creature clumsy.

Once the power word was uttered, the target creature started to feel a compulsion to be clumsy, ungainly, and awkward. The spell's effects affected the target's dexterity, making it hard for them to avoid attacks or perform actions that required nimbleness or sleight of hand. The length of the spell depended on the spellcaster's arcane prowess and vitality of the target creature. Some stronger creatures were completely unaffected by the spell.

Power word, maladroit required only vocal component to be cast.

In the, a copy of the spell could be found in the archive library of the Loremaster of Nimbre in Samarach, Chult.

## Portal well

Portal well was a rare transmutation spell.

Effects
This spell granted the caster the ability to wait inside a portal. It functioned by creating an extra-dimensional space within the path of the portal. The caster was capable of seeing both the entrance and exit of the portal as blurred images.

The spell would only function if the caster could activate the desired portal. Only the caster and their equipment could enter the extra-dimensional space. The spell expired once the caster left the portal at either end. If dispel magic or gate seal was cast on the portal while the caster was within the extra-dimensional space, they remained trapped there until someone else activated the portal. If the portal was destroyed by magical, such as with Mordenkainen's disjunction, or mundane methods, the caster was violently ejected from the portal.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component, a piece of silver wire in a knot.

History
Portal well was developed by clerics of Shaundakul.

## Port Kir

Port Kir, also known as the the Seacity of Tethyr, was a port city and center of guild activities in the nation. It stood along the Trade Way, between the towns of Mosstone and Zazesspur.

Description
Port Kir was a busy port with harbors being the town's lifeblood. It was significantly smaller, simpler, and impressively utilitarian compared to the bloated and splendorous ports of the southern merchant nations. Port Kir was scented by fish oil, and bigger Tethyrean settlements looked down on the Seacity, despite heavy reliance on fresh and saltfish produced by the port.

A tightly-packed big block of more than a dozen three-story buildings stood past the docks. These were warehouses and guildhalls belonging to many guilds of Port Kir. The biggest warehouse at the front of the block belonged to the Fishermen's and Fishmongers' Guild.

Geography
Port Kir was sheltered from storms and foul weather by the Dragon's Neck and Starspire Peninsulas, to the north and south, respectably. Calm waters of the Firedrake Bay welcomed the town's harbors stretching from the inlet where the town was built. It could hold up to a hundred vessels at a time. The Trade Way was a major trade route that allowed Port Kir to prosper. The road passed the town at the very edge of its borders. The portion of the Trade Way between Mosstone and Port Kir bore the name of Druid's Drive, named after numerous druids that dwelt in the forest along the road. The portion between Pork Kir and Zazesspur was referred to as Prince Piiclar's Road, named after a famed prince of the Strohm Dynasty.

Government
The governing in Port Kir was in the hands of the Guild Council, an assembly of Tethyrean guilds that moved away from the city of Zazesspur, where they lacked political influence, and moved on to Port Kir. There they were able to take control of the government. The takeover was gradual. Guildmasters earned seats on the city council. With time it became known as the Guild Council of Port Kir. With the guilds' control, Port Kir became the main trade power of Tethyr. Only one individual was above the Guild Council – the Lord-Mayor. Circa 1370 DR, the town's Lord-Mayor was Talmon Chonn, a puppet of Lord Paoltor Denaad's.

The rule of the Guild Council was unrestrained by the Queen of Tethyr. She did not stop them from wielding political and trade power; however, she supervised their activities to prevent market manipulation and insider trading. Those who were caught abusing the power of the guilds were arrested and taken to stand trial in front of the Tethyrean magistrate. Those found guilty were fined and barred from guild membership and politics for good.

Trade
Port Kir charged lower port docking fees compared to Zazesspur and Myratma. The average price was a single aenar. Naval businesses were the main industry in Port Kir. Its sailors and fishers were less brash and aggressive than those of Tulmene, and both cities often found themselves locked in bitter, sometimes bloody, rivalries. Numerous fishers, whalers, and shellfish trappers prospered in Port Kir. In the summer, fishing boomed, and the town's population grew drastically with seasonal workers, merchants, and visitors. During that time, Port Kir produced fresh, canned, cured, salted fish and shellfish. Another industry of note was shipbuilding. Lobsters, clams, and shrimp were also fished from the Firedrake Bay and considered a local delicacy. A unique species of star lobster was unique to Port Kir and was exposed to Zazesspur and Calimshan

Defenses
Port Kir and Mosstone shared garrisons and stationed them in Kirgard, the old manor of baron of Kirgrove. The mansion was ruined during the Black Days, but by the late 14 century DR, it was rebuilt and used for military purposes by the two neighboring cities. Guard patrols that secured the Trade Way were financed by Mosstone and Port Kir.

History
During the tumult of the, Lord Hhune-owned Berringer Shipyards constructed a private fleet of reinforced battleships that subsequently secured the neighboring Zazesspur's coastal holdings from pirates.

In the  the Reclamation Wars of Tethyr came to an end with Queen Zaranda Star's accession to the throne. Her crowning and blessing ceremony took place in Port Kir on Higharvestide of that year. The Archdruid of the neighboring town of Mosstone, the spiritual center of Tethyr, led the ceremony, and both groups of Tetyreans, coastal folk, as well as forest dwellers, accepted the new Queen as their ruler.

In the, Port Kir sent out a word to attract adventuring troupes with marine experience. Several fishing ships went missing in presumably safe waters to the west from the regular fishing areas. The town's government knew nothing about the disappearances but they knew that pirates were not to blame as there have been no buccaneer attacks on fishing vessels in ages. The culprits was the Split Mast tribe of seafaring orcs that recently claimed a lair on the shores of the Starspire Peninsula.

Sometime before the late 14, famed, or rather infamous, author Volothamp Geddarm became involved in an undisclosed "misunderstanding" resulting in him being barre from entering Port Kir or appearing within the town's patrol distance. This fact prohibited Volo from including Port Kir in his guide books.

As of the late 14 century DR, Lord Paoltor Denaad was plotting the assassination of Kyva Chaammos, his main political rival who earned the respect of the Queen Zaranda Star following the Reclamation Wars. If the murder was successful and untraceable to the treacherous Lord, Chaammos was sure to claim control of Port Kir. This plotting was not the first removal planned by Paoltor Denaad, as he was responsible for Kyva Chaammos' father's mysterious and sudden demise several years earlier. Paoltor inherited the plant to usurp dominance in Port Kir from his late father.

Notable Locations
Berringer Shipyards, the biggest business in Port Kir, owned by Lord Hhune in the late 14 century DR.
Dusty Throat, a rowdy and dangers tavern that served dockworkers cheap grub and bitter ale.
Gilded Dagger, a shabby old tavern used as the important traditional meeting spot for the guilds of Port Kir and visiting guild representatives of Zazesspur.
Race, a small dive bar on Firedrake Bay's waterfront, frequented by sailors and smugglers.

Notable Inhabitants
Notable Organizations
Fishermen's and and Fishmongers' Guild, the guild ran by the Finsire family. As of 1370 DR, the family has been on control of the Fishermen's Guild for several decades, and through the guild, the Finsires had hands in many of the aspects of Port Kir's daily life and governing.
Salters' and Packers' Guild, one of many guides in Port Kir that organized workers in the fish-salting industry.
Shadow Thieves' Sword Sect operated in Port Kir as well as in Zazesspur, Mosstone, and Wealdath.
Shippers' Guild, another guild based in the town's docks.
Thieves' Guild, local guild allied with Shippers', Fishermen's, Whalers', Salters', and Packers' guild. Each of the guilds' warehouses hid a concealed entrance to the Thieves' Guild.
Unified Shipmasters' Guild, Tethyrean guild that was present in Zazesspur and Port Kir.
Whalers' Guild, aptly named guild for whalers.

Notable Individuals
Aldegut, an adventurer from Port Kir who uncovered an enchanted sword after visiting the mysterious town of Blacbarn.
Havildar Oremmen, a thief from Port Kir who perished in Undermountain before 1358 DR.
Kyva Chaammos, the head of a rich salting and packing business owning family who was forced into the role after the death of her father.
Lantanna Indull Sarvann, a cleric of Deneir, cartographer and map merchant circa 1370 DR.
Myrindas, the Sage of Port Kir and the author of Dragons Ye Should Know published in 1354 DR.
Paoltor Denaad, a cleric of Iyachtu Xvim and a scion of a rich fishing and pearl-diving business family circa 1370 DR.
Reth Berringer, the alleged owner of Berringer Shipyards circa 1370 DR.
Talmon Chonn, also known as "the Walrus". A puppet Lord-Mayor of Port Kir circa 1370 DR.

## Potion of master thievery

Potions of Master Thievery were magic potions that fortified the imbiber's skills often associated with rogues and thieves.

Powers
When consumed, these potions temporarily gave the drinker abilities of a master thief, dramatically increasing their skills to pick locks and pockets. The effects of these potions lasted between three hours and thirty six seconds; their potency was reduced, depending on how skilled and powerful the imbiber rogue was. Potions of master thievery also temporarily improved the imbibing rogue's health.

Notable Owners
Black Lily, a merchant of Baldur's Gate's thieves' guild in the 14 century DR.
Silence, a merchant from the eastern part of Baldur's Gate  in the 14 century DR.
Vitiare, a person claiming to be a "master thief" in the town of Nashkel in the 14 century DR.

## Potion of shield

Potions of shield were enchanted brews that bestowed defensive magics onto the imbiber.

Description
Potions of shield were often bottled in ceramic or crystal vials, with enough liquid for a single dose. More common bottles used for potions of shield had a round bottom and long neck, sealed with a cork.

When consumed, potions of shield surrounded the imbiber with a magical protective field, similar to the effects of the shield and made them immune to the magic missile spell. The effects of the potion lasted for one minute.

## Potion of explosions

Potion of explosions was a volatile magic potion that exploded into a deadly ball of fire upon contact with air or physical disturbance.

Description
Potion of explosions were kept in safely-sealed air-tight vials. They were usually made of glass or ceramic materials and kept in shock absorbent frames or wrapped in a cloth to keep the glass from accidental breakage.

Despite its appearance as a seemingly regular harmless potion, drinking the concoction was not recommended. When uncorked, the potion had a high chance of exploding. If swallowed, a small amount of the liquid heated up the imbiber's insides, similarly to what drinking a potion of speed felt like. If the entire potion was consumed, the imbiber almost certainly entered the state of full system shock and exploded from the inside. If by some miracle, the explosion was not triggered, the imbiber fell extremely sick, unable to perform any actions for the period of time between three and six hours.

Powers
These potions were used as a bottled up slightly weaker versions of the fireball spell that upon impact, engulfed between  and  around it in flames.

History
As of the 14 century DR, potions of explosions were extremely rare due to the dangers of holding the end product and dangers of the brewing process.

## Porpoise

Porpoises were a species of aquatic mammal.

Description
The length of a porpoise ranged from  and they usually weighed between .

Behavior
Porpoises were known for being friendly and playful. They were also helpful.

Abilities
Porpoises were capable of holding their breath for a significant amount of time. Porpoises used sonar to see, using high-pitched sound waves that many creatures could not detect to see objects and other creatures to a distance of .

Ecology
Habitat
Porpoises were found in temperate climates and warm oceans.

Uses
Porpoises were a common companion animal to druids and rangers who lived in aquatic environments.They were also quite useful as mounts, as they could carry a medium sized creature on their backs even though they themselves where of a medium size. They could not, however, carry more than one passenger, and it was better for the rider to shed any unnecessary equipment to ease the strain of the porpoise. They could usually be bought for around 100 gp and it required an additionally 20 gp to train them.

Summoning
A celestial porpoise could be summoned by the summon monster I spell if one were in an aquatic or otherwise watery environment. With the summon monster II spell 3 celestial porpoise could be called uponA single ordinary porpoise could be summoned with the Summon nature's ally I spell.Likewise the spell Summon cetacean could call up to 16 porpoises to the caster, required that they all were within  when the spell was cast.

Magic
With the spell Cetacean form clerics of Deep Sashelas could assume the form of a porpoise.

## Power gauntlets

The power gauntlets were a project of Magister Horgyl Tusselvarr through the second half of the 12 century and the turn of the 13 century DR.

Description
Most of the power gauntlets were metal armor that reached all the way to the shoulder, with retractable spurs and claws.

Powers
The metal gauntlets were expected to allow their user, even if not a wizard, to cast spells. The spells invested in the gauntlet were thought to include cone of cold, delayed blast fireball, magic missile, repulsion, feather fall, fly, and levitate. Other powers likely included some sort of hammerblow-like physical attack, and a blast of something with properties akin to full sunlight, but not thought to correspond to any known spells as of .

History
As Magister, Horgyl Tusselvarr became soon engrossed with the task of creating those gauntlets during his tenure ( to ), which he explained to Azuth would allow wizards to leave someone else the use of blasting spells in dangerous situations, as well as giving rulers a taste of what magic could do for them. Azuth, for his part, stole six pairs of his most powerful gauntlets to better spur him into the creation of ever greater magic; unable to find out who stole them, Horgyl Tusselvarr created his final and most powerful pair, which was stolen before it was finished. Though the Magister chased the thief, he never realized it was Azuth.

## Pork

Pork, often called boar in Faerûn, was the general term for the meat of pigs and boars eaten across the Realms. It was a popular focal point of human and dwarf cuisines from Faerûn and Kara-Tur, and was also found in halfling and Zakharan cooking.

Description

The meat of pigs and boars had a number of different names depending on the type of cut and whether the meat had been cured. Popular discrete cuts of pork included roasts, chops, loins, and ribs. Cured versions, usually achieved with salting or smoking, were durable foods that would not spoil over the winter or during long trips. These included salt pork, bacon, and ham.

Whole Pigs

Whole animals were generally eaten roasted, and were referred to as "roast pigs" or "roast boars". They were popular at inns and taverns and were often the focal point of festivals and feasts in places like Damara, the Moonshae Isles, and Shou Lung.

Roasts
In Faerûn, pork roasts were often scored to allow for cloves or garlic to be embedded in the meat during cooking, while in west Zakhara they might be flavored with cardamom. In places like the Vast, roasts were often paired with carrots, lentils, peas, parsnips, onions, or squash. In the North and the Eastern Heartlands, roasts were served with things like green vegetables, potatoes, parsnips, turnips, and radishes.

Salt Pork
Salt pork was a cured preparation common in the Faerûnean Heartlands, the Korinn Archipelago, the Lands of Intrigue, and the North. It was common as a staple during long winters and as rations for adventurers, sailors, and soldiers. Salt pork's durability made it popular in places where pigs could normally not be raised, such as in the Underdark. Salt pork generally sold for about 3 gold pieces per .

Bacon

Bacon was a cured preparation generally found in northern and western Faerûn, and it was particularly common as part of a dawnfry or morningfeast. Bacon generally sold for about 4 gold pieces per .

Ham

Ham was a cured preparation usually made using the meat from the leg or hock. Ham generally sold for about 5 gold pieces per .

Stews
Pork was featured in hearty stews across Faerûn, paired with other meats, and with vegetables like onions, leeks, and greens as well as with spices. Boar meat was used in wildsage stew, eaten by farmers in the Vast.

Mince
The meat was sometimes eaten minced, often as a filling for dumplings, pies, pastries, and other dishes. Minced pork was one of the three meats most commonly found in a baeranth pie, in "jack wyvern tail" (meant to mimic the flavor of wyvern meat), and in tavern "steak". It was also a key ingredient in Delzoun meatballs, known as "tide me overs".

Sausage

Everything from fresh pork to pork fat to pigs' brains were used to make sausage. Often, these might be mixed with various other meats or other additions like nuts. Pig bladders were sometimes used as sausage casings. Pork sausage generally sold for about 1 gold piece per .

Other
Pork fat was used for cooking and baking. Rancid pork fat was boiled into a stew and fed to prisoners in Raven's Bluff. The fatty skin of the pig (pork rind) could be fried to make "cracklings".

In parts of northwest Faerûn, pig organs were used to make gravy and pig joints were eaten doused in gravy.

Pig's heads were a delicacy in Amn.

Availability
Pork was eaten across Toril, from dumplings eaten by the poor folk of Amn to suckling pigs at the tables of the nobility in the Cold Lands to roasts in the Pearl Cities of Zahkara to steamed buns known as "bow" in Kara-Tur to boar dishes in Laerakond. It was bought and sold, both fresh and cured, in city markets like those in Ravens Bluff and Arabel.

Pork was a common food at inns in Faerûn, and was often served spicy in the south (such as in Tethyr) and more mildly seasoned in the north (such as in Waterdeep).

Cured preparations of pork could be bought in bulk, and owing to their longevity, they were popular even in places where fresh pork was hard to acquire, such as in Skullport and Calimshan. Salt pork was one of the most popular exports out of Featherdale, where it was famously cured with minerals from the Deeping Stream.

Notable Locations
Inns, taverns, and restaurants notable for serving pork included:
 The Bargewright Inn in the Dessarin Valley served hearty but plain pork roasts with generous sides of potatoes, parsnips, turnips, and radishes.
 The Blushing Mermaid in Baldur's Gate was known to serve whole roast pigs as well as a pork platter with thyme and mushrooms.
 The Blushing Mermaid was also known to serve whole roast pigs, despite otherwise specializing in seafood.
 The Boar's Head Inn in Raven's Bluff was known as a place to find all kinds of pork products, from ham and leek soup to sizzling bacon.
 The Fall of Stars in Harrowdale Town served whole roast boar and a stew known as Boar and Chestnut Deep Pot.
 Hriiat Fine Pastries in Waterdeep was known for savory "bite-pies", including one filled with pork.
 The Inn of the Dripping Dagger featured several pork products, including chops and ribs for 1 sp, bacon and rolls for 6 cp, pork pies for 1 cp, and salt pork with mild or spicy beans for 4 cp.
 The Jovial Juggler in Beregost served whole roast boar.
 The Lonesome Tankard in Eveningstar served roast pork with boiled turnips and potatoes.
 The Old Skull Inn in Shadowdale served roast pork with boiled potatoes.
 The Owlroost Head in Purskul served dumplings filled with minced pork.
 The Painted Boat restaurant in Ravens Bluff offered barbecued pork as a specialty
 The Pampered Traveler inn in Waterdeep made various roasts (including pork) available buffet-style in the mornings for its guests
 Phoaraudo's in Raven's Bluff served pigs' ears as a delicacy.
 The Raging Lion inn in Waterdeep made two pork stews nightly, one highly spiced and one meant to allow the flavor of the meat to shine through.
 The Sailors' Own tavern on Sail Street in Waterdeep served roast pork as well as pork stewed with onions and greens.
 The Stag Triumphant in Mosstone served whole roast boar.
 Starnar's Cozy Cove Rest in Tulmene served a spicy pork stew that was thick, sticky, and brown.
 The Stone Stallion in Amphail served pork roasts every third night, drenched in fatty gravy and accompanied either by green vegetables in summer or potatoes and parsnips in winter.
 Tulmene's Tower in Tulmene used minced pork as a stuffing for their signature preparation of giant squid.
 Two Brothers' Butchery in Raven's Bluff sold a wide array of pork products, including specialty cuts and pig carcasses.
 Wyrmkindler Sausage Works in Cormyr used venison, pork, and pork fat in their stag sausage.

Pork could also be found for sale at the Nowhere Inn on the Infinite Staircase.

History
Pork was a staple food in north Faerûn since the days of ancient Netheril.

High demand among the nobles of Chessenta made pork one of their most important imports as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR owing to the fact that few pigs were raised in the realm itself.

In the late 15 century DR, the merchant Alsimur Droon manufactured a shortage of salt pork along the Sword Coast, allowing him to capitalize on the desperate demand.

Trivia
The flavor and texture of pork was said to be similar to the meat of wyverns, rothé,  and subterranean lizards. Pork when flavored with chives was also said to simulate the taste of stirge meat.

Pork was one of the favorite foods of the average beholder. Perytons were not over fond of it.

## Portal of Plenty

The Portal of Plenty,  also known as Chauntea's Blessing, was a portal connecting a Chauntean shrine in Archendale to a different world. It was used by the followers of the Earthmother to trade agrarian goods and knowledge with this world's inhabitants.

Location
The portal could be found in a shrine dedicated to Chauntea, located in a village of Archendale. It was known by many across the Dales, but outsiders believed the shrine to be a regular center of worship.

Structure
The portal was  wide and about  high. It was flanked by a pair of columns engraved with images of farming and of worship.

Activation
The portal could be activated for 6 seconds by touching a stock of wheat against the carvings on the columns. Once activated, anyone or anything could pass through.

History
During the mid–14 century DR, Chauntea granted a vision to some of her followers inhabiting another world, causing them to visit Toril. They arrived in Archendale, where they discovered that the local farmers worshiped the same goddess as they did, only using a different name. Consequently the Portal of Plenty was built to connect both worlds, allowing the faithful to trade agrarian goods and knowledge between worlds. This caused Archenfolk to acquire agricultural knowledge far ahead of their time.

However, in the early 1370s DR, the community of the other world was decimated by the assaults of a lich lord leading undead and fiendish armies. Monsters consequently started spewing from the portal, until a wizard was hired to close it using the gate seal spell. The spell was later dispelled, leading the Dalesfolk to believe a traitor was in their midst.

Some sages believed that the lich lord was trying to conquer Archendale as a stepping stone to attacking the rest of the Realms.

## Pouch

A pouch was a container used for carrying small items, such as coins, on one's person. They were commonly used as adventuring gear. Those who used slings most often kept their sling bullets or stones in pouches, and pouches were also how children's marbles were typically stored.

Description
Most pouches were made of leather and weighed about . Those made for small folk, such as gnomes or halflings, were about a fourth the size and weighed around . Most could strap to one's belt. They sold in most markets for around 1 gold piece but could be found in Aurora's Emporium for only 2 silver pieces. Merchants selling sling bullets usually included a complimentary pouch with the bullets.

Variations

 Feedbag A pouch used to feed domesticated animals.
 Seed pouch  This large leather pouch was worn over the hip and used by sowers to carry up to  of seed. They were sold by Aurora's Emporium for 2 gold pieces.
 Spell component pouch  This pouch was watertight and had special compartments for quick access to tiny material components and foci for spells.
 Spice pouch  A type of leather, drawstring pouch that was used across Toril to carry little glass vials of spices, encased in whittled wooden 'shielding shells' to guard against breakfast. These pouches had a "finished" side as their interior, so as to minimize any reactions with the contents of the vials.
 Waterskin  This pouch had a narrow neck and was used for carrying water.

Notable Pouches

There were many magical pouches known to have existed in the Realms:
 Bag of boulders  This pouch contained sling bullets that would grow to the size of light catapult stones when thrown.
 Bottomless pouch  This pouch contained an extradimensional space, allowing it to store more than it should have been able to physically.
 Dahlver-Nar Pouch A leather pouch decorated with stitched images of various heroes and lightly fanciful creatures was typically used to carry the Teeth of Dahlver-Nar.
 Enemy spirit pouch  This pouch was worn like a necklace by rangers and enhanced their skills against their favored enemies.
 Girdle of many pouches  This magic belt contained multiple extra-dimensional pouches that could respond to mental commands from the wearer.
 Gnomish workman's pouch Sets of Gnomish workman's leather were built with 56 pouches that functioned similarly to a bottomless pouch or pouch of accessibility. Only sixteen of these pouches were visible, with five of them having nine additional pouches sewn into their interior and each of those fifty could hold up to  of material weighing up to . Five of the unconnected pouches could only hold up to  of material weighing up to . And one larger pouch was designed to hold a familiar, regardless of its size, along with any food or water they might need. All of the pouches responded to their wearer's thoughts, always having on top whatever specific item was desired by them when they reached in.
 Heward's handy spice pouch  This magic pouch could create any spice that the owner desired.
 Pouch of accessibility  This pouch magically contained 30 separate interior compartments.
 Pouch of winds  This magical bag contained wind that could blow a sailing vessel.
Spell-Made Pouches
Some spells in the Realms allowed casters to create pouches.
 Wrap  Pouches were among the many types of containers that this cantrip could create from any material. Any pouch created by the spell could hold a maximum volume of  and could open by the spellcaster issuing a verbal command.

Trivia
The word for "pouch" in thieves' cant was byb.

## Power key

Power keys were incredibly rare tools or methods created by deities to allow divine spellcasters to circumvent the planar restrictions on divine magic that existed in the Great Wheel cosmology. That being, an increasing weakening of their abilities for every plane of existence that seperated them from their deity's divine realm.

Description
The forms that power keys could take were nearly limitless, from physical objects to even gestures or rituals. However, the form was usually representative of the deity that created it. For example, power keys of Garl Glittergold were usually gold or jewelry, those of Flandal Steelskin were usually small hammers engraved with flames, and those of Ilmater usually took the form of a strong blood-red cord that had to be wrapped around a user's wrist.

Powers
A power key was imbued with part of the divine strength of the deity that made it, its creation having cost them a sliver of their power, This was speculated by sages to create a link to the very source of a deity, allowing one to draw directly on their energy of existence. However, this same link meant that power keys were subject to the whims of their creator and could be changed without warning.

Under normal circumstances, power keys didn't just restore normal functionality to clerical spells, but made them work even better than before and reach their maximum potential. However, many worked only for limited durations, especially those made by chaotic deities, having been intentionally designed that way as a safe guard against loss or theft.

They could be divided into three types: specific, general, and greater.
Specific The weakest kind of power key, these were tied to only a single spell and were the most likely to have no limited duration.
General These power keys improved all spells of a single sphere of influence.
Greater The most powerful and rarest of power keys. These allowed for far less loss of clerical power due to planar distance from a given deity's home plane. The strongest of these were usually reserved for proxies on urgent missions.

Additionally some evil deities, usually chaotic evil ones, delighted in making false power keys. These altered spells in undesirable ways, perverted them, or outright canceled them. Sometimes even without the caster's knowledge.

Varieties
 Among the powers of Arcadia, power keys were usually highly regulated. Clangeddin Silverbeard gave out power keys that were golden battleaxes, mithral hammers, and miniature replicas of anvils. The Mulhorandi powers Isis, Osiris, and Ra gave out power keys made of silver or petrified wood and in the shape of an ankh or scarab.
 The power keys of the Gnome pantheon's members varied in what spheres they granted access to, but virtually all granted access to the Elemental and Protection spheres.
 Ilmater was generous with power keys. He usually granted access to the All, Healing, and Protection spheres.
 Laduguer, being a god of crafts, gave out power keys that were items that had been forged by the recipient and blessed by a duergar priest. These keys only functioned for their crafters.
 Malar, being a god of the hunt, gave out power keys that were animal claws or fangs.
 Among the powers of the Norse pantheon power keys were most often gifted out by Frigga, Heimdall, Loki, Odin, and Thor. These five typically gave keys out only to priests that they deemed worthy, though as a god of thieves Loki notably allowed any power keys that were stolen from him to function. The various other powers of Ysgard gave out their power keys on a whim, with the most freely awarded granting access to the Combat and Guardian spheres. The power keys of Odin governed mental spells, such as the various extension spells and Rary's mnemonic enhancer. Loki's power keys were all related to illusions and deceptive alterations, such as alter self, delude, mirage arcane, and vacancy.

Availability
Power keys were passed out by deities to those who were their most favored, faithful servants, having earned them through years of service. And usually this gifting was done through a proxy. However, there were cases in which deities would gift them to receive a servant's aid in direct and dire services, such as the quest to liberate Waukeen from imprisonment in the Abyss.

They usually could not be bought or traded for, though this did not stop some from trying to pass off forgeries, like the patrons of The Sodden Solar in the Abyssal city of Samora. And on the wartorn plane of Acheron, some military officers were willing to sell theirs for a proper bribe, usually a minimum of 1000 gp, though unscrupulous officers were likely to sell keys close to expiring.

Notable Users
 Annia Graystockings, a paladin of Ilmater, carried a power key in the form of a wooden carving of two bound hands.
 Khamallan Teren, a proxy of Malar.

Appendix

## Potion of song

A potion of song was a potion that gave its drinker incredible singing abilities.

Powers
By consuming this potent potion, an individual could produce a stunning singing voice, regardless of any impediments such as being gagged, affected by magical silence, or having lost their vocal cords or tongue due to injury or natural form. The imbiber had complete control over the pitch and timbre of their voice, ranging from a high, ethereal soprano to a deep, resonating bass, which could be adjusted at will for the duration of the potion's magic.

Upon consuming the potion, the individual became impervious to all sound-based assaults, including the banshee's piercing wail, the entrancing song of a harpy, and the deafening roar of a sphinx. Moreover, the potion also bestowed upon the imbiber the ability to flawlessly replicate the vocal tone of any creature whose voice they had previously heard.

The individual's voice could be projected across distances of up to, filling vast halls or other large chambers with ease, and even penetrating through the deafening clamor of howling winds or the cacophony of battle. With some experimentation, the singer could learn to modify their potion-enhanced song.

The potion also allowed the individual to convey messages through their song lyrics and captivated the attention of all sentient beings. Even an inexperienced singer could expect to earn a considerable sum of money in a marketplace or tavern by delivering a remarkably emotional or unforgettable performance.

## Portis Alvermantle

Portis Alvermantle was a Sembite merchant and spy who operated in Cormyr in 1369 DR.

Description
He was a stout man with a red face, soft hands, and rich clothes, marking him as a prosperous merchant.

Personality
Portis was a nervous man, but had a good sense for when he was in danger.

History
In the, he spied on the Dragoneye Dealing Coster and the Seven Suns Trading Coster in Cormyr on behalf of the rival merchants of Yhaunn. On the way home along the Hullack Trail he stopped at the Journey's Rest inn during a storm, only to intuit something was terribly wrong despite the friendly atmosphere provided by the beautiful innkeepers Ryanna and Moria. Though he did not know precisely what the danger was, he packed up and left that night despite the weather. This saved him from being devoured by innkeepers, who were both secretly vampires.

## Porb Piiradost

Porb Piiradost was a member of the Piiradost noble family of Waterdeep in the late 15 century DR.

Description
He had graying dark hair and dark eyes. He wore silver robes, a belt, and rings beneath a green cloak.

History
In the late 1490s DR, Lord Forge Fitzwilliam of Neverwinter revived the High Sun Games, a blood-sport in which adventurers would battle monsters in a maze in the newly built Neverwinter arena. Porb Piiradost was one of a number of rich, powerful, and unscrupulous people who came to bet big on the Games and brought their riches with them. However, they needed a secure place to store their riches. To this end, Forge offered the use of Castle Never's treasure vault, but Porb and the others wished to see its security for themselves. Thus, Forge his advisor Sofina showed Porb Piiradost and Din Caldwell around the castle, boasting of its upgraded security and suitability for storing their wealth in its vault, which was protected with a powerful Mordenkainen's arcane seal. Porb had to ask why Forge had revived the games, but Forge deflected with a joke. However, they were interrupted when Sofina sensed the presence of a wild shaped druid—Doric—spying on them, but she escaped Sofina and the castle guards.

Nevertheless, Porb and the others trusted their wealth to Forge Fitzwilliam. This would prove to be unwise as Forge stole it all and, during the High Sun Games, tried to abscond the city with it. Meanwhile, Porb and the others bet on the contestants and the monsters; Din idly asked Porb if he'd ever eaten displacer beast flesh, but Porb declared it too gamey for his liking. Both their fortunes were later lost, scattered over the streets of Neverwinter for the common people to collect. But Porb was not to know because, while he was watching and betting on the Games, Sofina revealed herself as a Red Wizard of Thay and used the horn of beckoning death, turning him and all the gamblers into zombies.

Appendix
Background
Porb Piiradost was played by actor Paul Bazely.

The credits of Honor Among Thieves spell his name 'Piradost', with one 'i', but as a wealthy person of Waterdeep, he is most likely a Piiradost, so this wiki assumes he is a member of the family and adopts that spelling instead.

## Pork fat

Pork fat, sometimes in the form of lard or bacon fat, was the fat of pigs and boars. It was generally for use in cooking.

Description
Pork fat was popular for culinary applications in places like Cormyr, the Dalelands, the North (although there it was not as popular as goose or goat fat for cooking), Tethyr, the Vast and the Western Heartlands. The lard of wild boars was generally interchangeable with that of domestic pigs.

Pork fat could be either liquid or semi-solid, requiring that it be scooped. It was perishable and thus often stored in the same environments as pork or vegetables, but it could also be packaged in casks for transport, such as on ships. It was generally quite flammable.

Culinary Uses
In cooking, pork fat was measured by the "knife". It was often used to cook or fry other foods, such as fish, game fowl like quail, mushrooms, onions, and potatos, Ladling liquid pork fat over meats as they roasted on a spit—causing the fire beneath to roar—was a particularly decadent preparation method. Pork fat was also used as an ingredient in dishes ranging from high-class pâté to low-class bloodlick, as well as in sausage and as an ingredient used to make bread or pie and pastry dough.

In places like Raven's Bluff, rancid pork fat was sometimes boiled into a stew to feed to prisoners.

It was said that some elves had a form of rations that were patties made of corn, dried fruits, nuts, seeds and small candies that were held together by lard, honey, molasses and/or sugar.

Other Uses
In magic, a pinch of lard was used as a material component in the grease slick spell. It could also be used by alchemists and druids in the making of ointments by mixing it with dry or caky substances to allow it to be applied to the skin.

Pig fat could also be used as a lubricant. For example, it could make a smooth surface slippery, such as for setting a trap similar to a use of oil of slipperiness. Grapplers could also apply it to their skin to give them an edge against being grabbed, and in a pinch, pork fat could be applied to a naked human to allow them to squeeze into spaces intended for smaller folk. Some sages suggested applying a coating of pork fat over exposed skin prior to battling a steel dragon or black dragon in order to protect from the effects of the beasts' breath weapons.

Availability
Pork fat could be found in pantries and larders across Faerûn.

Notable Locations
Inns, taverns, and restaurants notable for their use of pork fat included:
 The High Moon Inn in Highmoon used it as a cooking medium.
 Hot Highsun Handbreads in Gladehap used pork fat in their rabbit pate.
 The Watchful Eye in Essembra used it when making the dough for its famous stuffed turkey pie.
 Waukeen's Rest on the Risen Road served potato wedges fried in pork fat.
 Wyrmkindler Sausage Works in Gladehap used pork fat as part of the filling in their stag sausage.

Rumors & Legends
There was said to exist a wondrous item known as the everful jug of lard which could provide an endless supply of pork fat for cooking.

Appendix
See Also
 "Boar fat", another term for bacon.

## Port Nyanzaru/Warehouse District

The Warehouse District of Port Nyanzaru was a section of the city's Harbor Ward where goods being shipped into or out of the city were stored.

Description
The district was characterized by its canals that separated the rows of warehouses. Full-size merchant ships could be towed through these canals by dinosaurs, allowing for the vessels to be brought straight to the sites where their cargo was to be loaded or unloaded. Because traffic jams were a notable concern with this arrangement, organizing this towing operation was one of the key responsibilities of Port Nyzanzaru's harbormaster.

Geography
The Warehouse District was located in central Port Nyanzaru, east of the drydocks and west of the public docks.

History
In the late 15 century DR, the harbormaster in charge of traffic management in the Warehouse District was Zindar.

## Potash

Potash was a manufactured substance that was widely used in the Realms.

Availability
Potash was widely produced around Faerûn, in a process that involved soaking the ashes of wood fires.

The Bedine of Anauroch produced and exported large amounts of potash, as well as the derivative soap and glass. The processes to make these were held as secrets by certain Bedine tribes, who did not believe that wider Faerûn knew how to produce potash, glass, or soap. If an outlander professed to know such a process, they were killed to protect the "secret".

Usages
Potash was used in a large array of techniques, including the production of glass, soap, and textiles, including felt, silk, and menga cloth.

## Potion fruit

Potion fruit was a general term for enchanted fruits and berries on the desert world of Athas, which took the place of potions and magical oils as water was too rare and valuable a resource there to waste on such usages.

Effects
The magical effects of a potion fruit were contained within its juices, which had to be ingested or allowed to run over the user's body in the case of oil-type potion fruit. This had to be done within one minute of breaking the fruit's skin, beyond which these magical properties were lost, and they lasted for a period equivalent to a potion or oil on other worlds.

Potion fruit had a greater shelf-life than their base fruit, remaining ripe for strictly 99 years before rotting normally. Enchantment did not cause any change in taste from the base fruit, though potion fruits and the trees they grew upon could be identified with detect magic, identify, and similar spells.

Varieties
There existed potion fruit equivalents for all manner of potions that were common across the Multiverse, such as apples that acted as potions of healing, cherries that acted as potions of bull's strength, pears that acted as potions of restoration, or persimmons that acted as potions of levitation.

Creation
A potion fruit could be created by a cleric, druid, or wizard by enchanting mundane fruit in much the same way they would create potions and magical oils. Alchemists could achieve similar results through strange forms of herbalism. Alternatively, they could be grown by clerics, druids, wizards, or rangers by planting a whole potion fruit in the ground and then tending to the tree it grew within a period of one to six weeks. Once potion fruit was picked from a tree it would produce no more, though such trees could be made to produce more by casting permanency on them. However, a potion fruit tree subjected to permanency had a maximum lifespan of 99 years.

The type of fruit enchanted had no effect on magical properties. Though fruits that were drier and thus lacking in ample juices, such as dates, couldn't be made into potion fruit.

History
In The Year of Despair, Malatran calendar, late 14 century DR, a group of heroes of the Malatran Plateau were sent through an elemental gate to the far-away world of Athas. Created temporarily by Roshink, the elemental noble explained that he and his fellow nobles fled from this world long ago and it is what could befall the Malatran Plateau should the heroes fail to vanquish the dreaded Tamara. In order to return home, he instructed them to head for the mountains until they came across a great forest, where they would find an "oak tree that is too big for a saru to put his arms around". When they found such an oak, he would be able to bring them back to the Plateau through the Dreamlands.

Immediately upon arrival, the group was captured by Athasian elf slavers. who confiscated any standard potions they had brought with them from Toril as mere water supplies. A young thri-kreen named Ka'cha was among creatures captured by the elves, and he lamented being separated from his clutch. Subsequently, the heroes escaped when the elven caravan was attacked by a hungry sink worm. They seized the opportunity and freed Ka'cha, fleeing from their pursuers across the desert.

After two days of travel across the hot sands, guided by Ka'cha, the group stumbled across his tribe, the Windrunners, and their leader, Ki'rik'ku. Ka'cha convinced them that the strange adventurers were friends, not food. Happy with one of their number returned, chief Ki'rik'ku gifted the aliens four large erdlu eggs (an invaluable source of nourishment in the Athasian desert) and two apples of healing. The thri-kreen then explained to the Malatran heroes how to reach their destination—the Ringing Forest—the way back to Toril.

While on the way to this forest the adventurers came across a mul village built around an oasis. There they were offered a number of trade goods, including potion fruit such as cherries of bull's strength, pears of restoration, and a persimmon of levitation.

Appendix

## PomFritte Fromage

Sir PomFritte Fromage was an eccentric hin adventuring knight of the Golden Rooster from the Living City, Ravens Bluff, active in the late 14 century DR.

Description
PomFritte was a pompous swashbuckling halfling who spoke with a distinct accent – pronouncing "the" as "zee."

Relationships
Sir PomFritte Fromage's "snugglebunny" was named Chickadee.

History
In Uktar, Nightal of the, PomFritte Fromage announced the formation of the so-called Halfling Nation. The knight's reasoning and plans for the new-fangled nation were laid out in the interview conducted by Jennylou Brightleaf of the Ravens Bluff Trumpeter broadsheet. The interview occurred in Sir Fromage's large suite in the Seven Tigers Inn. The knight stated that elves and dwarves formed cities, towns, and nations of their own, unlike the hin (strangely forgetting about the existence of Luiren). However, he did not propose that halflings leave Ravens Bluff and start a farming community elsewhere. The Bluff has all the amenities the eccentric knight wanted, food was acceptable, and the lodgings were tidy. When asked what laws this proposed Halfling Nation would follow, Sir Fromage declared his commitment to the Golden Rooster and the city's laws. The halfling ended the interview as he needed to supervise the construction of his wine tasting room and closed it by declaring that all haltings who were interested in joining the "nation" were to stop at the Seven Tigers Inn and book a room using the special Halfling Nation rate.

## Pondmuk

Pondmuk was a goblin member of the Legion of the Chimera in the early 14 century DR.

History
In the, the town of Targos in Icewind Dale became besieged by the Legion of the Chimera. One of the Legion's surprise attacks involved teleporting goblins from underground tunnels into the Targos docks. The next attack came as a direct assault on the town's palisades. Lord Ulbrec Dinnesmore of Targos ordered a group of mercenaries from Luskan to follow two of his scouts, Ennelia and Braston, southeast to the goblin fortress. Vunarg and his tribe resided in the recently erected fort in the frigid Icewind Dale tundra.

Pondmuk, and his tribe, under the leadership of Chief Ogrok, was among many goblins who used the caverns underneath the fortress during the Legion's war with the Ten Towns. Goblins and Pondmuk, in particular, were bullied by the Legion's orcs and deprived of food. When Lord Ulbrec's mercenaries infiltrated the fortress, Pondmuk showed no hostility towards them and only asked for their help – to bring him big fat corpses of delicious giant spiders to eat. Pondmuk offered information on the Legion and the fortress in exchange for the spider corpses. He also stated the password to get into the hobgoblin part of the fortress – "chimera."

## Polymorph (spell)

Polymorph was an arcane transmutation spell that allowed the caster to transform into another creature.

Effects
When cast, the spell affected a single targeted creature within  of the caster. If the targeted creature was unwilling and was strong enough, there was a chance they could resist the transformation caused by this spell. Any creature with the innate ability to shapechange could resist this spell at will.

The targeted creature was transformed into a beast determined by the caster. The polymorphed creature retained their personality and alignment but their mental abilities and health became those of their new form. They also faced any natural limits of their new form. Communication through speech, spellcasting, and actions that necessitated hands or speech were unavailable to the targeted creature. The targeted creature's belongings merged seamlessly into its new form, rendering it unable to utilize, activate, wield, or derive benefits from its equipment. The targeted creature remained polymorphed until the spell expired or they were killed.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component: the cocoon of a caterpillar.

## Portrait of Shandra

The Portrait of Shandra was a painting created by Pepin Pollo of Neverwinter in the late 14 century DR.

Description
The Portrait of Shandra was a masterfully painted image of Shandra Jerro, a one-time companion of the famed Knight-Captain, sometimes referred to as Kalach-Cha. Shandra's image looked past the observer, and her face held a beautiful light smile.

History
In the, gnomish painter Pepin Pollo traveled from the City of Splendors, Waterdeep to the Jewel of North, Neverwinter, where he made coin by painting portraits of those who could pay for the service. One day, the person who would later be known as Knight-Captain came across the diminutive artist. Shandra Jerro was one of Kalach-Cha's new companions, and her beauty instantly captivated Pepin. The painter shooed away the woman whose portrait he was working on and immediately began creating the Portrait of Shandra. From his inspiration was born a beautiful and masterful painting Pollo called his masterpiece. The Knight-Captain purchased the painting for Shandra; however, it left her possession soon after when Shandra was murdered by her grandfather – the infamous warlock Ammon Jerro.

## Porrenath

Porrenath, the Second City of the Sword, was one of the first major cities in the empire of Jhaamdath.

Geography
The Second City lay a short distance northeast of the First City, Naarkolyth, on the southern edge of the Orsraun Mountains. After its destruction, it lay in the Selmal Basin beneath the waters of the Vilhon Reach.

History
Porrenath was founded in, becoming the second of Jhaamdath's major cities.

A period of bloody imperial expansionism started in the, which resulted in the logging of the Chondalwood and the massacre of the Nikerymath elves. The elves responded in the  by performing a High Magic ritual that created a great wave from the Inner Sea that tore out of Jhaamdath Bay and washed over the Cities of the Sword, carving out the Vilhon Reach in the process. Porrenath remained a sunken ruin hidden in the Reach, with its many psionic artifacts slowly leaking into the water and affecting the surrounding environment.

The sunken lands of Jhaamdath were suddenly revealed by dropping sea levels following the Spellplague in 1385 DR, becoming the Vilhon Wilds. This was almost as quickly reversed in 1486 DR by the Great Rain and Second Sundering hiding the ruins once more.

## Polly's All Night Pastry Emporium

Polly's All Night Pastry Emporium, or simply Polly's, was a bakery, café, and pastry catering business that operated in Ravens Bluff in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Location
Polly's All Night Pastry Emporium was a several-story-tall building, with the shop taking the first floor and rental apartments above it. It was located two streets west and half a mile north from the Jack Jaw rowdy drinking establishment. At night, the street on which Polly's stood was illuminated by a pleasant red radiance of street lights.

Interior
The shop's interior felt a bit out of place among the establishments that surrounded it. The dining room of the café was plush with velvet fabrics and massive woven rugs and throws. The room was made out of dark hardwood. The dark wood floors were covered by comfy rugs and hardwood furniture was upholstered. A warm fireplace illuminated the room with a large birdcage hung above.

Services
The cage was the office, of sorts, where the Emporium's original proprietor–Polly, could be found. The large green parrot with an eyepatch was the establishment's mascot who greeted guests in human speech, periodically squawking like an ordinary parrot would. The parrot did her best to run the Emporium out of her cage despite having difficulties with writing and reading. She sold the bakery's pastries to the guests and fulfilled catering orders that were prepared by two teams of employees who worked day and night shifts.

History
Polly's All Night Pastry Emporium was in operation for several decades by the late 14 century DR. Its owner, Polly, once made a deal with the powerful cleric of Tyr, Seejil. She sold him a lion's share of the Emporium for the price of most of the cleric's savings. Part of the deal was that in case Polly was ever killed, she would be brought back to life and to health by Seejil. Sometime later, Polly died in a horse carriage accident, but Seejil was unable to resurrect his business partner because of a misadventure that made Tyr turn away from the man, stripping Seejil of all of his divine magic. Thankfully, Seejil convinced a druid friend to use the reincarnation spell on Polly, technically fulfilling the agreed-upon obligation. She was reincarnated as a big green parrot.

Several years after a group of adventurers helped to redeem Seejil's name, Sorduel Meytauc of the Ill Eagle Inn, bought out Polly's pastries to pass them as his own in a competition at the Mossbridges's First Annual Year-End Festival in the . "Coincidentally," Polly's own invitation to partake in the competition disappeared at the same time. Sorduel Meytauc entered Polly's dark chocolate cherry cake but did not win the competition due to being found out and embarrassed by a group of adventurers of the Living City.

## Potion of female form

Potions of female form were magic potions that changed the imbiber's body to that of a female of their species.

Powers
When consumed, a potion of female form transformed the drinker's body into that of a woman. It was believed that the potion's effects lasted only for twenty-four hours. However, that was not always the case. There was a ten percent chance that the transformation was permanent and could only be reversed through the wish spell.

History
In the late 14 century DR, the Church of Selûne in Ravens Bluff sought to retrieve and destroy the obsidian amulet of the Dark Maiden – the prison of Jessar, the dark goddess enemy of Selûne. The amulet could only be handled by a woman equipped with a special silver gauntlet in order to avoid being possessed by the deity. All men who touched the amulet were immediately destroyed, and their souls consumed. The Temple of Selûne assembled groups of adventurers of the Living City to send them in search of the artifact. Those groups lacking females were offered these potions, promising temporary effects lasting only a day.

## Polyhedron A/Ravens Bluff Rumors

Ravens Bluff Rumors is a one-page adventure for Dungeons & Dragons 1 edition printed in Polyhedron A as an introduction to the Living City organized play program and its central city setting of Ravens Bluff.

Synopsis
In an unnamed settlement boarding the Sea of Fallen Stars, a group of adventurers short on coins decide to try booking voyage on a ship up to the Dragon Reach and its port city of Ravens Bluff, feeling certain that the Living City is a place to find adventure and earn a quick coin. Finding themselves unable to afford passage on large ship, the adventurers try looking for a cargo vessel to sign up for work as a man-of-arms. They come to find that all large mercantile vessels are full on crew members, leaving a small cog known as The Black Scalawag as their only option.

While conversing with people around the docks and on the Black Scalawag itself the adventurers are told a wide variety of rumors surrounding recent attacks on ships. Some claim a furious storm giant is to blame, sinking all ships in retaliation for dumping garbage into the waters and not stopping until he receives reparations. Others claim a giant squid is attacking vessels, a whirlpool to another plane of existence is swallowing up ships, or that the deity Istishia is sinking all ships whose crew members don't worship him. These all turn out to be tall tales.

Some speak of increasing attacks by sahuagins wielding various armaments. While others tell of reports of seals near ships moments before they were sunken and of a lone survivor of a recent shipwreck being rescued. He reported to his sailor saviors that the ship he had been on was attacked by werewolves, then disappeared during the next full moon. In actuality the recent string of attacks on ships was being perpetrated by a pack of lesser seawolves, who had infected the sailor with their form of lycanthropy. They in turn were being regularly followed by a tribe of sahuagin, who took advantage of their attacks to scavenge shipwrecks and then attack lone ships on their own.

On the second night out at sea two men keeping watch disappear on The Black Scalawag and on the third another two go missing. On the fourth night there are no disappearances, but on the fifth four men mending the ship's sails are reported as seemingly jumping overboard. By the sixth day there are only seven people left on the ship, plus the adventurers. The Black Scalawag's captain is in a panicked state, uncertain what course of action to take, for there are no signs of other ships in the vicinity and they are too far out at sea to try turning around. Looking around for any clue as to what was causing the disappearances, the adventurers find only a few spots of blood and scraps of fur. When the sun sets that day it's the group of adventurers who are chosen to take watch and they come face to face with the lesser seawolves.

If any of the adventurers have some means of traversing the watery depths, they might come to find the lair of the seawolves and the treasure therein. Within their horde a trove of 22,000 copper pieces, 16,000 silver pieces, and 700 gold pieces are found. As well as fourteen gemstones, each worth a hundred gold pieces, and a +2 short sword. When they bring it all aboard The Black Scalawag their captain insists upon them sharing the loot, as he wishes to compensate the families of the eight crewmen that were lost to the seawolves.

## Polyhedron 81/Renegade's Run

Renegade's Run is a short adventure set in Ravens Bluff for Dungeons & Dragons 2 edition, published in Polyhedron #81. It is designed for one or more players of 2 to 4 level.

Summary
On a night when the fog phenomenon known as The Veil was creeping heavily into Ravens Bluff, Romilys Ganelon, a sergeant in the City Guard and a trusted aide of the Chief Constable with a past history of burglary, steals a prized bronze horn of Valhalla belonging to Lord Marshal Gaius Varro. Romilys is spotted entering the sewers, but the guardsmen are unable to effectively pursue him, being that they are all spread out in stations designated for nights of heavy Veil activity. Ten minutes later a desperate Rolf Sunriver runs into the barracks of the Night Watch and enlists the aid of those inside to capture Romilys.

Showing them a partial map of the sewers, the Chief Constable explains that Romilys is also carrying a ring of free action and a skeleton key, giving him an even greater chance of escaping. He estimates that the watchmen have roughly twenty minutes to determine Romilys's path and get to his expected exit ahead of him. Before they depart, Rolf hands the watchmen a ring of spell storing that Gaius had loaned him, holding only the spell invisibility, 10' radius. He recommends they utilize it to set up an ambush at his expected exit. When the ambush eventually kicks off, Romilys tries to fight his way out and uses the horn of Valhalla to conjure a group of berserkers to fight for him.

## Pool of Loss

The Pool of Loss was a gate linking a cavern beneath Waterdeep with Myrkul's City of the Dead when it stood in the plane of Hades.With Myrkul's City of the Dead becoming first Cyric's City of Strife and then Kelemvor's City of Judgment and being relocated to the Fugue Plane, the status and function of the Pool of Loss following the Time of Troubles are unknown.

Description
On the Toril side, the Pool of Loss lay within a vast cavern similar to a cathedral in scale, with its high ceiling and far ends obscured in darkness when mundane torches were used for lighting. The pool itself lay in the center of the chamber. It was small and ringed by a stone wall, rather like a well.

When it was sealed by Midnight, a shimmering rainbow-hued dome (the upper half of a prismatic sphere) encased the pool, entirely fitting the walls of the well.

Geography
The cavern lay deep beneath Mount Waterdeep and was a part of the greater Undermountain complex. The known route there was circuitous: from inside the shaft of the second "wet" well in the Yawning Portal inn, one could enter a side passage of the maze of natural caves and dwarf-dug tunnels under the mountain. Altogether, these twisted and turned, sloped up and down, and intersected other tunnels, making it difficult to map. Moreover, some of these passages had partly collapsed and others were half-flooded.  The whole cave system was dark, dank, and dripping. Lastly, a steep tunnel or dirt-walled chute descending some  and curving sideways led down to the cavern in which the Pool of Loss was found. Those who slid down the chute were propelled safely into the cavern, though the timid might tie a rope to a rock and climb down.

Access
The gate to the Realm of the Dead was usually left open, allowing 'soul spectres' to go to and fro. These took the form of glowing white translucent figures and could move and fly, see, and even speak, but were completely intangible whilst on the Prime Material Plane, so neither they nor the living could attack one another.

At the end of the Time of Troubles, however, the gate was sealed, first with a permanent prismatic sphere cast by Midnight, and then another by Elminster. This blocked all passage to matter and magic, and even to the spectres, which were annihilated on contact with the sphere.

History
In the ancient history of Waterdeep, the barbarian tribes of the North that settled the lands above used this pool as a burial site, throwing the bodies of their greatest leaders into its waters. They believed this ensured their greatness would continue to serve their people.

During the Time of Troubles on Marpenoth 14, Elminster, Khelben "Blackstaff" Arunsun, and the adventurer Kelemvor visited the Yawning Portal to meet Durnan and the dwarf Undermountain guide Gower. The mages hired Gower, for the price of two-score mugs of ale, to lead them to the Pool of Loss so they might reach the Realm of the Dead and find Midnight and the second Tablet of Fate. Led by Durnan and Gower, they went down the inn's wet well and through the tunnels, finally sliding into the cavern. They found the area swarming with hundreds of trapped spectres and a blinding prismatic sphere covering the portal, correctly presuming it to have been cast by Midnight from the other side to block invasion by the forces of Myrkul, the god of the dead. Elminster dispelled the first layer of the sphere and dropped his familiar pipe inside as a signal to Midnight. Leaving the remaining sphere intact, Midnight emerged and rejoined Kelemvor and Elminster and they all returned to the Yawning Portal to confer.

The next day, however, Myrkul came and destroyed the rest of the prismatic sphere, allowing his fiendish minions, called the denizens, to march through. They passed through the tunnels to the sewers and invaded the Dock in the Battle of Waterdeep. During the battle, Elminster returned to reseal the Pool of Loss, reasoning that the fiends would lose some of their powers if cut off from the Realm of the Dead. He succeeded and the defenders of Waterdeep gained the upper hand. In the aftermath, Khelben retrieved Elminster from a new sphere over the Pool of Loss, where he'd been beset by denizens.

## Pool of Daylight

The Pool of Daylight was a pool of liquid that glowed with radiant energy as bright as a sun. It was hidden away in a chamber in the depths of the Temple of Filth in Evernight on the Shadowfell.

Description
It was said that an immensely powerful artifact lay within the Pool of Daylight which had the ability to annihilate undead creatures by the hundreds.

The light of the radiant pool was enough to cause crippling pain to any undead who entered it chamber. Touching the liquid would instantly destroy lesser undead, including weaker ghouls and vampires.

Defenses
The Pool was protected by the ghouls of the Temple of Filth, and was locked behind cursed and trapped doors deep within that Temple. It was kept shrouded by rituals of darkness.

History
The truth of what lay in the Pool of Daylight was known only to the priests and the Tribunal of Evernight. They claimed that a powerful artifact was stored there to keep it out of the hands of Evernight's enemies, but also to hold it in reserve if they ever needed a doomsday weapon. As of the, the mere threat of this artifact was used to dissuade the Thayans from trying to conquer the city, although this was largely unnecessary because the Thayans were satisfied with their arrangements within the city.

## Porcelain doll

A porcelain doll was a type of doll crafted from the material known as porcelain.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold porcelain dolls, shaped solely like humans, that were made in the cities of Tilverton and Urmlaspyr. For a time the catalogue also sold dolls that had been made in Westgate, until it received numerous complaints about the poor quality of Westhavian porcelain. Advertised as being perfect for the children of retired adventurers, these dolls came in rich swaddling clothes and were sold for 2 gold pieces each.
 The Toysmiths, a toyshop in Ravens Bluff that was owned and operated by the gnome brothers Gunder and Relvan Gaewilder, sold porcelain baby dolls. They offered male ones for 2 gold pieces and female ones for 25 silver pieces. Customers were asked to specify what color they wished to have for the eyes and hair of their doll.
 Hezza, a pawnbroker in Lyrabar, carried porcelain dolls in his shop.

Notable Owners
 Maniacal Manny, an enchanter living in Procampur, owned a menacing looking porcelain doll of a brown weasel.
 Some male merchants in the Realms collected and traded porcelain dolls in secrecy, as they were liable to face ridicule by their acquaintances or outrage from their wives over how much they spent on such dolls. Some rented out private locales to get together for 'swap meets', such as at the House of Twilight in Tantras.

## Poisonchops

Poisonchops was a giant spider from the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, Poisonchops, along with other giant spiders, drow, and duergar, all were slaughtered in the home of Bloomridge Park groundskeeper, Lavernica. The murder spree was performed by Orin the Red and her Murder Tribunal of Bhaal.

## Polton Karrick

Polton Karrick was a painter of some social standing from the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
As of the, Lady Jannath was one of the patrons of Polton's. She purchased at least one painting from the artist – a still life picture, that Polton was failing to deliver on time. Some time later the same year, Polton Karrick was invited to attend the art show titled "Strange Tides: Self-Portraits from the Sword Coast," hosted by Lady Jannath in her Lower City estate. However, the lady's butler–Tarhun Mnemonis, was instructed to gently reminder the artist of the delay.

## Porklet

Porklet was a play penned by a famed half-elven poet and playwright Archibald Ravenshade in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Porklet was a story about an irresolute man who wrestled with a decision to murder his evil uncle.

History
As of the, Porklet was immortalized in the form of a painting by Oskar Fevras. The image, painted in the late 15 century DR, depicted Archibald Ravenshade, surrounded by two of the Vanthampur brothers, as the half-elf unveiled a review of a theatrical production of Porklet. The painting, simply named The Vanthampur Brothers, was stored in Oskar's studio in Lady Wisteria Jannath's Lower City manse.

## Potent word of nurturing

Potent word of nurturing was an advanced utterance from the Lexicon of the Evolving Mind that used truespeak to rejuvenate a target from its wounds, or to rend them apart, if reversed.

Usage
In its straight form, healing energy suffused the target, slowly repairing them of a massive amount of damage. In its reversed form, it rended and tore at the target's connection to its body, dealing grievous wounds. It could last for an instant, but with some concentration, damage would be doubled after a few seconds.

History
The utterance was known to logokron devils, garblers, who used only the reversed form, and loquasphinxes.

Appendix
References

---

## Potato chowder

Potato chowder was a simple variety of potato soup made in the Realms.

Description
The recipe called for a large soup pot to be greased with lard in which aromatic vegetables, such as leeks, garlic, and onions, were to be sautéed. The vegetables were seasoned with salt and drizzled with wine. The alcohol and vegetables were cooked until the wine was reduced. The potato wedges were added and cooked until soft. All contents of the soup pot were then mashed together with various dairy products like cheese and milk.

History
In the mid-to-late 15 century DR, a potato chowder recipe was recorded and published in the Soups of the Realms by Brother Donnick, the priest cook from the Open Hand Temple dedicated to Ilmater, the Crying God, located in the Rivington district of Baldur's Gate.

## Pol (houndmaster)

Pol was the beloved Chief Houndmaster of the Durinbold patriar family of Baldur's Gate, who died sometime in or before the late 15 century DR.

Description
In life, Pol had long wavy hair and wore a trimmed beard.

Possessions
He used a crossbow in battle, in life and as a ghost.

Pol's sarcophagus held a magic scroll of the animal friendship spell and a potion of animal speaking.

History
By the, Chief Houndmaster Pol was long dead and interred within the Durinbold Mausoleum of the Lower City Graveyard, surrounded by the interred bodies of his favorite beasts, Gordis, Rocky, and Zina. The trainer and his three dogs were ghosts that would attack anyone who dared to invade and disturb the Durinbold family tombs.

## Porter Juxon

Porter Juxon was a human commoner from the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Porter was a human man with light skin, gray eyes, and black curly hair. His black mustache and mutton chops were properly groomed and shaped.

History
In the, during the threat of the Cult of the Absolute, Porter could often be seen arguing with hin friend, Bliggerillo, over the Baldur's Mouth gazette's reliability and its claims that the walls of the Gate would protect its citizens. Bliggerillo showed no confidence in the broadsheet's propaganda, unlike Porter. The human believed that the broadsheet would never lie to the entire city.

.

## Popkin Shortshanks

Popkin "The Shiv" Shortshanks was a roguish and gnomish adventurer from Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Popkin was a pale-skinned woman with bright green eyes and reddish-brown hair kept tied back. She wore mismatched hide armor.

History

Around the, Popkin and her fellow adventurers Jarus, Helayna, Liantor, Kirina, and Tristran discovered an ancient tomb at the bottom of a crevasse in the Stonelands. They fought their way past some undead, and found a hidden passage leading deeper underground. The group pressed on boldly, but were ambushed and overwhelmed by the kobolds of Clan Daeronnax. The fighting separated all the members of the group, and though Popkin escaped she was unable to find her friends again or fight past the threats that filled the caves.

She returned to Eveningstar in search for help, and received a number of magical trinkets that contained word of recall spells keyed to Eveningstar, from a "nice old gent with a weird hat" that she met in town. She looked further still for someone to deliver the enspelled trinkets and save her friends, and found some capable adventurers that were willing to help. She shared what she knew of the situation and directed them onward in her ox-pulled cart, anxiously waiting to see if any of her friends lived.

Although Liantor Stoneborn had already been killed, thanks to Popkin's actions her other four companions were able to be rescued and safely recovered from the caves. This even included Tristran, who had been kept for a snack by the red dragon Daeronnax.
