## Poison spore

Poison spores were a substance extracted from myconid organs – pockets of poisonous gas, that evolved to be their defense mechanism in the cruel and unyielding Underdark.

Usage
Poison spores sacks were valued by alchemists, who used them to produce noxious spore grenades. This thrown poisonous bomb-like weapon was the result of combining myconid poison spore sublimate (a sickly-sweet vapor distilled from the spores) and certain types of alchemical ashes.

## Pock-Marked Po

Pock-Marked Po  was one of the so-called "beggar princes" of Port Nyanzaru in Chult, making him an influential figure in that city's underworld in the late 15 century DR. He was known as the Hideous Prince, and sometimes used Tarenbiril as an alias, meaning "hideous prince" in Elvish.

Description
Po was a Calishite man with dark hair and dusky skin. His body was significantly deformed by a serious illness. Po had reduced eyesight in one of his eyes, and his joints had become inflexible. His hands were deformed into gnarled shapes, and he had a stooped gait owing to trouble walking unaided. He dressed in a frayed cape.

Personality

Po was very crafty and had a quick wit and a silver tongue. He had a level head, and a polite demeanor.

Activities
As a beggar prince, he was one of the unofficial rulers of Port Nyanzaru's Old City and the thieves of the city listened to his commands.

He was connected to the Zhentarim and acted as their principle contact in the city, although his superiors allowed him to operate mostly autonomously. However, he was responsible for protecting their secrets and improving their reputation.

He sponsored a team of competitors in Port Nyanzaru's dinosaur races known as the Bladefangs.

Relationships
Due to his poor health, Po was attended to by a number of young Chultan women who cared for him, fed him olives, and helped him with things that his body did not allow him to do.

Po had many contacts throughout Port Nyanzaru, through which he conducted his affairs. His subordinates among the Zhentarim included Henry Hallowhall, Zansha, Jaha Mubsie, and Rokah.

Among the Merchant Princes of Port Nyanzaryu, Po respected Kwayothé but disliked Jessamine and Wakanga O'tamu.

He generally strove to ensure that the Zhentarim seized favorable opportunities out from under rival factions,  however he occasionally cooperated with representatives from such other factions within Port Nyanzaru, such as when several of them elected to hire the mercenary Pozzanna Lordeaux to secure a stronghold within the Chultan jungle, when they rallied behind Hawkdriver Mazugulak to drive back an undead attack on the city, or when they came together under Alastar Bol to send agents to investigate undead forces stemming from the ruined city of Matolo.

He had a son named Basu, although he outwardly cared for him relatively little compared to his "bigger family" of the Zhentarim.

Possessions
Po lived in a lavishly decorated and perfumed hut that had a dilapidated exterior, in the south end of the Old City. However, in his capacity as a beggar prince, he held court in the smallest of the three step-pyramids overlooking Executioner's Run.

History
At one time, Po had been an adventurer, however he was left deformed after a disease nearly killed him.

As of the events of the death curse around, Po was in his mid–30s and worked as an agent of the Zhentarim. During this time, he tasked a group of adventurers with participating in a dinosaur race in the Market Ward in order to win the Oracle's Eye, a piece of a Dreamer's Amulet. He also hired adventurers to investigate the disappearance of a beloved local tavern owner, Miboro Yan, and personally oversaw the near-execution of a witness, Favin Kreeger, to learn what had happened to her.

When Port Nyanzaru came under attack by the fiendish forces of Idrianne Smoke, Po's thieves waged guerilla war against them in the Old City. The Merchant Prince Kwayothé sought to strike a deal with Po that would see him ally with her mercenaries.

When his son Basu disappeared on a trip to Fallen Feather, Po sent adventurers to find him and the information that he carried.

When the Red Wizards of Thay threatened Chult, Po hatched a plan to create "proof" of the Zhentarim's superiority to goodly factions. He planned to send agents against the Red Wizards whom he would order to behave especially "heroically" and have record their deeds with a memory crystal.

## Ponderous Tome

A Ponderous Tome was a magic book that likely fell into the possession of several adventurers in the late 15 century DR.

Description
This thick book had a cover made from some brown material. It was decorated with golden yellow symbols and clasps. It had a yellow gem on its spine.

Powers
Using the item made a chosen person think deep, remorseful thoughts. This could be used to distract opponents and interrupt their attacks. However, this ability had varying recovery periods before it could be used again.

History
In the late 15 century DR, Ponderous Tomes could be found in Neverwinter, Gauntlgrym, Mount Hotenow, and Neverwinter Wood. They were also available for purchase at shops in those locations. Such books were likely found by the adventurers that stopped Deathsleep, the green dragon.

## Poisoner's Phial

Poisoner's Phial was an apothecary shop in the gate-town of Plague-Mort.

Location
This shop was located in the Merchants' Quarter of Plague-Mort, one of the gate-towns of the Outlands.The 4 edition sourcebook Demonomicon details this shop and the town of Plague-Mort as being within Pazunia, the 1 layer of the Abyss, though as this is not a Realms book and no other Realms-relevant sources acknowledge this, it can be firmly considered non-canon.

Services
This shop sold a variety of poisons, venoms, and their affiliated antidotes, as well as acids and medications. Some of these antidotes were mundane, whilst others were magical in nature. Antidotes were sold for twice that of what the shop charged for the toxins they were designed to neutralize.

History
Around the mid-to-late 14 century DR, a group of adventurers from the city of Ravens Bluff visited the gate-town of Plague-Mort and while there may have shopped at the Poisoner's Phial.

Inhabitants
This shop was owned and operated by a male tiefling by the name of Laran Susspurus.

## Power word, disable

Power word, disable, was a powerful spell from the enchantment school that could be wielded by both sorcerers and wizards.

Effects
After the Year of Wild Magic, 1372 DR, the spell was able to either bring an opponent to the brink of death or make them collapse unconscious. In both cases, the vital energies of the target were brought to near full depletion.

The spell only worked on creatures, under a certain average degree of health, whether they could hear it not. Creatures more robust were unharmed. The target had to be close to the spellcaster.

Components
The spell required only a verbal component, the uttering of the power word itself.

Usage
As of the Year of Lightning Storms, 1374 DR, the spell was used by people in Rashemen and Samarach, and on the Dragon Coast and Sword Coast North.

## Power word, deafen

Power word, deafen, was a powerful spell from the enchantment school that could be wielded by both sorcerers and wizards.

Effects
After the Year of Wild Magic, 1372 DR, the spell was able to deafen a creature permanently or temporarily. The length of the effect was dependent on the target's health. The weaker the creature, the greater the risk of it being deafened permanently.

The spell only worked on creatures, under a certain average degree of health, whether they could hear it not. Creatures more robust were unharmed. The target had to be close to the spellcaster.

Components
The spell required only a verbal component, which was the uttering of the power word itself.

Usage
The spell could be wielded by both sorcerers and wizards.

## Power word, liquefy

Power word, liquefy, was a powerful spell from the conjuration school that could be wielded only by wizards.

Effects
Upon casting this powerful spell, one or more creatures in a chosen area were instantly turned to water.

The spell only worked on one creature with weak health or several with a very small degree of health. Creatures more robust were unharmed. The chosen area had to be close to the spellcaster. If the spell was cast underwater, the volume of all creatures liquefied caused no displacement and the liquid dispersed with time. The estimated volume of all creatures acted as normal fluid. Creatures affected were forever slain and could only be re­stored by a wish or an act of the gods.

Components
The spell required only a verbal component, which was the uttering of the power word itself.

Usage
The spell could only be wielded by wizards. It was considered a very rare spell.

## Potion of command slimes

Potions of command slimes, also referred to as potions of slime control, were a form of potion that afforded imbibers some degree of control over various forms of oozes.

Powers
The effect of a potion of command slimes was comparable to the spell command plants when cast by a wizard of the 8 degree, but applied to puddings, slimes, and molds instead of vegetation. The active duration of this effect was around 10 to 40 minutes."Molds" in this case most likely refers to the monstrous molds of the time – brown molds, russet molds, and yellow molds.

History
Sometime in the late 14 century DR, a group of Ravenian adventurers were gifted a potion of command slimes and six gemstones worth 400 gold pieces by the retired adventuring bard Luff as an award for breaking the curse that had been placed upon his lyre twenty-five years prior by the wizard Leland.

## Poniard of pain

The poniard of pain was a magical poniard dagger held by Karissa the Knife, Master Thief of the Night Brigade in the late 15 century DR.

Powers
The blade left particularly deep wounds and chilled the victim with a numbing cold, but its true value was in its adaptive nature. It innately targeted the vulnerabilities of those it struck, hitting angels as if evil, demons as if good, fey as if cold iron, werewolves as if silver, and so on. The wounds inflicted by the dagger refused to heal normally.

History
The poniard was in the possession of the greedy thief Karissa in her lair until she was slain by the Riverside Rats gang.

## Potion of eagle's splendor

Potions of eagle's splendor were common magical potions that increased the imbiber's charisma.

Powers
Potion of eagle's splendor greatly increased the drinker's charisma and affability, similarly to the effects of the eagle's splendor spell.

History
By the, these potions could be found in wide use in many places across the Sword Coast, such as Neverwinter, Daggerford, and as far as the Nelanther Isles and Icewind Dale.

 Lucia Calefar, a drow rogue of the Night Masks in the late 14 century DR.

## Postal service

A postal service was an amenity available in some parts of the Realms, sometimes provided by local businesses, as well as couriers and runners, trained pigeons, or more exotic magical means. These sporadic services were generally available locally, while longer-distance mail and parcels were delivered via traveling peddlers, minstrels, caravans, and trading coster offices.

Description
Most mail was in the form of written messages, short verbal messages, and small packages, all delivered for a significant amount of coins for the common folk, rarely less than 6 gold pieces paid upfront. The price included a possible third party making the final legs of the delivery. In contrast to the land folk, aquatic elves used heralds and messengers to deliver oral messages in place of physical letters.

Letters were sent in sealed envelopes, while packages were wrapped in two layers of canvas, sewn, and sealed with pitch or sap to protect the contents from dampness. Depending on the written letters' contents, the senders often utilized codes and ciphers due to the lack of privacy along the long delivery routes.

Churches employed their own messaging systems that operated independently from outside involvement or used magical means to transport reports, messages, written prayers, and orders. This service was made available to worshipers who have made significant donations and tithes to the church in the past, however, the service was not free but done at a reduced rate.

Official communications made by the crown and other rulers were delivered by heralds.

Notable Postal Services
 In the nations of Cormyr and Sembia, one could hire armed couriers who operated within the borders of their respective realms to make mail deliveries for letters and small parcels. The population density made this a lucrative business, with deliveries costing 25 gold pieces or more.
 In Suzail, Tathlin's Discreet Errands functioned as a courier service in the 15 century DR. Those who were interested in deliveries of small packages sought out Tarpreskur's Conveyances, which also offered taxi services.
 Arabel's own letter service was called The Eyes and Ears of Arabel, which also offered caravan guard hiring and parcel deliveries.
 Woody Glen, a small town on the northwestern edge of the High Forest, had a small parchment makers' shop that also served at the town's post office. The shop's workers collected mail to be sent out of town and received and sorted incoming letters.
 Dwarves ruled by Bruenor Battlehammer established a relay team of couriers connecting Mithral Hall to the town of Mirabar.
 Sword Coast Couriers operated in the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR, utilizing couriers, trained dogs, and mail pigeons.
 The underground city of Skullport used Spider's Fleet o' Feet to deliver verbal messages, written letters, personal missives, and small packages. Its competitor was Haffa's Flatbacks, which employed kobolds to wheel parcels across town.
 The aptly named Raven Express was the mail service found in the city of Ravens Bluff. It used trained ravens to carry and deliver small, wax-sealed, scrollcase parcels to various cities throughout the Vast and north Faerûn. Another package delivery service that operated in the Vast was Ungar's Parcel Service of the town of Sendrin.
 Khôltar, a city in south Faerûn, used the services of Halamor's Sure Service, which employed hin runners to carry messages, supplies, or packages but operated strictly within city limits.

## Polyhedron 69/Sea of Fire Part I: River of No Return

Sea of Fire Part I: River of No Return is an adventure module set in Kara-Tur. It was published in March 1992 in Polyhedron 69 in 1992. It was followed by Sea of Fire Part II: The Sea of the Problem. The adventure was designed by Robert Farnsworth and is intended for 4-6 characters of 7-9 levels.

Synopsis
A powerful wu jen from Wa slew the chiang lung guardian of Lake Pakarsh and re-routed the Tokali River via a gateway of symmetry. This caused deadly draught in the Hordelands, harming the wildlife, the nomad tribes, and starting grassfires. The Khans tasked a group of adventurers to investigate the drying Tokali River. In a race against the clock, the adventurers were joined by Pok Luing, a chiang lung sent by the Celestial Emperor to replace the killed dragon as the lake's guardian. They discovered the gateway of symmetry and followed Pok Luing through the gate to Wa.

## Pompton Norvath

Pompton Norvath a wealthy citizen of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Pompton was a human man with a dark skin tone, and light brown hair. Wore a quality coat and a big leather hat.

History
In the, Pompton Norvath lived in the Lower City, strolling the streets near a shady part of the town with abandoned buildings where the notorious Guild had one of their Guildhall entrances guarded by the gang's thugs and snipers. Pompton wished that area was more secure via steel watcher patrols and warned passersby from peeking into the abandoned buildings' windows in fear of sniper arrows. He could often be seen with one of his neighbors – Delyarna.

## Potion of digestion

A potion of digestion was a variety of potion that greatly expanded an imbiber's consumptive capabilities.

Powers
Those who imbibed this potion were granted not only the ability to digest any organic substance, all without suffering any deleterious effects, but all such substances became immediately appetizing to their palette. However, the potion did not enhance any part of the consumptive process beyond digestion - if an individual wanted to eat a plank of wood for example, they would have to manually break it down into consumable pieces. The duration of a ‘’potion of digestion's'' effects could lasted anywhere from one to four days.

## Poke (lycanthrope)

Poke was a female wereboar of the Black Blood tribe from Caer Moray on Moray in the Moonshae Isles during the late 15 century DR. She was killed in the late 1470s DR while battling drow on the island of Gwynneth.

Description
In her human form, she was slightly pudgy, with pink skin and pale yellow hair on the crest of her head.

In her hybrid form, she appeared mostly like a large and hairy human woman save for her sharp, hoof-like hands and feet and her bestial face (with small eyes, wide and distorted nose, and curved tusks). She spoke in grunts with a low and distorted voice in this form.

In her pig form, she looked like a large, mundane pig with tusks. Her powerful molars could crush bones.

In all forms, she bore an intricate tattoo of a yellow rose inked in many colors under one arm. It was designed to look like the Rose of Sarifal, and was modeled after the tattoo on her friend and leader, Lady Amaranth.

Personality
She was modest, and empathetic. Having been raised in part by Lady Amaranth—a leShay princess—her manner of speech was overly formal even as it was punctuated by pig-like grunts. She was considered clever (albeit forgetful) by the standards of lycanthropes.

Poke had been raised by Amaranth to respect her humanity more than her bestial nature, and as such, she hated the feeling of losing her intelligence as she transformed into her pig form. She felt most natural in her hybrid form, but assumed her human form in front of strangers or while out in daylight.

Relationships
She was a trusted friend and servant of Lady Amaranth, a young leShay princess who had been marooned on Moray and come to lead the lycanthropes of Caer Moray. Poke loved and admired Amaranth endlessly, and it was Amaranth who had named her.

History
In the late 1470s DR, Lady Amaranth became increasingly desperate to return to her home of Sarifal on Gwynneth, from where she had been abducted a decade prior. However, her adoring followers would not allow her to leave, even going so far as to sink a boat she had intended to use. Thus, she instead entrusted Poke with traveling to Sarifal in her stead in order to find her half-sister, Ordalf, and to ask for her help. Poke thus built a boat to please her, and she and several others embarked on the journey across the Strait of the Leviathan to Gwynneth. However, as they departed from Kork Head, the boat was attacked by fire nagas, who destroyed it and left Poke as the only survivor. She drifted in the cold water for hours and through a storm before she was discovered by the fey of Sarifal.

Poke was taken to a hidden prison beneath the ruined city of Caer Corwell, where she soon came to realize Ordalf in fact wanted Amaranth dead. She was interrogated and tortured for information about Amaranth, but although she refused to talk, her tattoo was evidence of her association with Amaranth and Ordalf recognized that she had clearly come from Moray. This was sufficient for Ordalf to hire the crew of the Sphinx to find and kill Amaranth, as well as to imprison one of them, the gnome Suka, alongside Poke as an added incentive for the crew. By this time, Poke had befriended another fellow prisoner, the fomorian Marabaldia, and had naively resolved to escape and seek the help of the Winterglen Claw, a rebel group opposed to Ordalf.

Poke, Marabaldia, and Suka soon became friends, and bonded over an overly melancholy song that Suka would sing, known as Oh, Father Dear. Suka devised a plan for their escape, and a tenday after her arrival in the prison, the trio overwhelmed their guards. However, they were then confronted by the leShay Prince Araithe, who (like his mother Ordalf) was also interested in learning from Poke where to find Amaranth. The trio attempted to fight him, but his magic confounded their minds. Only by transforming into her beast form, in which her mind worked more on instinct than thought, could Poke avoid the effects of his magic. She swiftly defeated him, shattered his arm, and dragged his body out of the prison where she and Marabaldia decided he should be spared, despite Suka's objections.

Fleeing from Caer Corwell, the group was attacked by drow in service of Prince Araithe, but escaped with the aid of the Winterglen Claw, who had also learned of Poke's arrival and apparent association with Amaranth. Poke was questioned by Captain Rurik as well as Lord Mindarion, Amaranth's paternal uncle, and told them about where Amaranth had been and what she had been doing in the ten years since she had disappeared from Sarifal. However, her tale was interrupted by a surprise attack from the drow, and Poke was fatally injured by a darkwalker. Although she was obviously dying, Marabaldia insisted on helping to carry her to the rebels' hideout at Harrowfast. As her life faded, Poke slipped into her pig form, and when her corpse was laid to rest in the sepulchurs of Harrowfast, she was indistinguishable from a simple pig.

Meanwhile, Amaranth remained oblivious to Poke's trials, although as the days wore on, she feared the Poke had forgotten her mission or else her crew might have mutinied. It was not until Amaranth herself arrived on Gwynneth that she learned of Poke's death from Suka and Marabaldia, and this confirmation of the loss of her friend provided the young fey with a strong reminder of the need to sever her attachments to mortals.

## Pomerian

Pomerian was a powerful Thayan necromancer who became a lich beholden to Grintharke, the balor ruler of Hellgate Keep, in the 14 century DR.

Personality
As a lich Pomerian was arrogant and complacent from the power he wielded, convinced he could make no mistakes. And due to his enthrallment to Grintharke, the lich was more than willing to fight enemies to the death, as his balor master could always construct a new body for him.

Abilities
In addition to the powers granted by lichdom, Pomerian possessed a spellbook with an extensive collection of spells. These included the following:
 animate dead, armor, Bigby's clenched fist, blink, burning hands, chain lightning, chill touch, clone, cloudkill, color spray, conjure air elemental, contagion, control undead, curse, darkness, 15' radius, death spell, delayed blast fireball, detect invisibility, detect undead, dig, dimension door, disintegrate, dismissal, dispel magic, energy drain, enervation, extension I, ESP, feign death, finger of death, fireball, flaming sphere, flesh to stone, fly, haste, hold portal, hold undead, ice storm, identify, knock, levitate, lightning bolt, locate object, magic jar, magic missile, massmorph, monster summoning I, passwall, polymorph other, power word kill, power word stun, prismatic sphere, prismatic wall, protection from good, reincarnation, shatter, slow, spectral hand, strength, summon shadow, telekinesis, trap the soul, true seeing, vampiric touch, web, and wizard eye.

History
After an attempt to seize more power for himself nearly cost him his life, Pomerian left Thay and journeyed for some time, until finding an ally in the Cult of the Dragon. Their relationship was a means to an end, with Pomerian simply interested in gaining knowledge on prolonging his life. Once he had what he wanted, Pomerian traveled further north to the Savage Frontier, where he would come to be acquainted with Grintharke.

Discerning what Pomerian’s true goals were, the balor resolved to provide the necromancer the last bits of information he was missing to undergo lichdom. Pomerian set about preparing a hidden for his lichdom ritual, thinking he was being  furtive, but in reality his movements were being monitored by Grintharke’s spies. On the day of the ritual, when the last of Pomerian’s life force was seeping into his phylactery, Grintharke jumped in and soul the vessel, teleporting it away to a hiding place he had specially prepared and whose location he had magically obfuscated. Grintharke then returned to the cave and explained to Pomerian that he was to be in his service, until such a day came when the balor felt like giving the phylactery back.

Around the,The Savage Frontier dates the start of Nanathlor Greysword's reign to 1313 DR. The Pipes of Doom state he has been the leader of Loudwater for over 45 years. Taken together, this would place the date of the adventure to 1358 DR. Pomerian led the forces of Hellgate Keep through the Underdark and back out to the surface in a surprise attack on the town of Llorkh. They were comprised of a horde of various orc tribes, a quarter of which were worg cavalry, 18 ogres from the Greypeak Mountains, 13 hill giants likewise from the Greypeaks, and 19 elite drow troops from Menzoberranzan. Using the Pipes of Doom, a powerful magical item that Grintharke's former master had created, he was able to route Llorkh's forces before they had a chance to repulse the invasion.

The Hellgate army then set their sights on the town of Loudwater, though its ruler Nanathlor Greysword was preparing for their attack, as he had been forewarned of it by a half-orc spy by the name of Barnabas. However, their duplicity did not go unnoticed - Pomerian had discovered the half-orc was a double agent well before they made their ascent on Llorkh. Rather than eliminating him right away, Pomerian decided to keep Barnabas around and fed with nominal intel, reasoning that the spy could be exploited to create diversions.

When the Battle of Loudwater commenced, Pomerian darkened the fair skies by summoning thick, foreboding clouds with the Pipes of Doom''' control weather power. He then used spell scrolls to cast mass invisibility on his drow forces, enabling them to attempt a stealth attack on the enemy command post. Finally, the lich took to the center of the horde, flanked by ogres, and proceeded to play the Pipes of Doom to induce a steady retreat from the forces amassed at Loudwater.

Which side was victorious in this battle was ultimately uncertain, though the odds were firmly stacked against the forces of Loudwater and they were said to have suffered staggering losses. With a draw in the conflict, the Hellgate army set up camp for the night near the South Wood, working to construct huge rafts with which to cross the otherwise impassable River Shining.

Shortly after the night's evenfeast, while Pomerian plotted away the next day's strategy within his command tent, a group of korreds snuck into camp and stole the Pipes of Doom. Once Pomerian realized they were missing, he sent a force of elite individuals out into the woods to recover the stolen Pipes and kill the thieving korreds. Lead by the fire giant Slag, they included the drow Lilliana, George the gorgon, and roguish twin princes Dammek and Dammon. The spy Barnabas took this opportunity to slink off to the opposing camp and tell Nanathlor of what had transpired. This gave the forces of Loudwater new found hope, for if they could retrieve the Pipes of Doom before the Hellgate army and destroy them, Loudwater would have a chance at routing the invaders. And so a group of renowned adventurers set forth on this quest.

When Barnabas returned to the Hellgate camp, he was swiftly detained and subjected to an ESP spell. Through this they probed his mind for what had just went down back at Nanathalor's camp. Seeing no further use for the spy, Pomerian killed Barnabas and then left to join his units in the recovery of the Pipes of Doom.

When he caught up to the group Pomerian informed them of how their situation had altered. Slag then carried him the rest of the way to their destination. Sources differ on which group arrived first to the circular clearing of the korreds, where they were using the pipes as part of their ceremonies of dancing, but all agree on the tactics employed by the Hellgate party.

Pomerian covered himself in dust of disappearance, then cast upon himself fly, as well as levitation as a back-up precaution. He then enlarged his body, guarded it with protection from good, and enhanced his senses with ESP and detect invisibility. Lastly, Pomerian hasted his party and backed up their assault by summoning forth six hobgoblins and six shadows.

The chieftain of the korreds was particularly singled out by the lich, who conjured an air elemental to attack him. When the adventurers intervened and had it destroyed, Pomerian resorted to casting true seeing and spectral hand, then through that spectral hand attacked the most powerful of the adventurers with the spells burning hands and vampiric touch''. And if this battle dragged on for long enough, Pomerian was prepared to unleash his most powerful spells with reckless abandon, no longer caring if harm befell his allies in the process.

As the town of Loudwater did not fall to the forces of Grintharke in 1358 DR, the adventurers presumably won out against Pomerian and his lackeys.

## Porcupine

Porcupines were a nocturnal mammal that lived in many forests.

Description
They were between  and  long, with thick muscular tails, and a back covered in spines. The black porcupines tended to be on the larger side, while the brown was on the smaller. Their quills had overlapping barbs at the end which made them difficult to remove and often caused painful swelling even after disposal.

Behavior
Being very timid and prone to panic, they were relatively non-aggressive unless threatened.

Combat
When attacked, porcupines would swipe with their tails, dislodging quills into their opponents and then attempt a retreat away from their attackers.

Ecology
As they were herbivores, porcupines fed on the bark and leaves of trees, while fruits and salt were delicacies for them.

Habitat
They were noted to live in many places in Faerûn, from the great Cormanthor forest, to the Tun Marshes, and many a settled land.

Relationships
Porcupines were sometimes used by brambles as mounts.

Famous Porcupines
 Ambitious, a porcupine in Marigold's Menagerie in the 14 century DR.

Appendix
See Also
 Giant porcupine: a far larger and dangerous version of this animal.
 Hedgehog: a smaller creature that in many ways resembled the porcupine.
 Quills: porcupine-like creatures native to the Outer Planes.

Porcelain golems were a rare variety of golem made from porcelain. They were more ornate than other forms of golem.

Description
Porcelain golems were very large animated constructs. They were white in color and decorated with a blue floral pattern.

Behavior
When a porcelain golem was surprised, they blinked. When it was prevented from the task given to it, it moved violently and made loud roaring noises.

History
In the, a porcelain golem was given the task of protecting the Sun Gem in the Blade Kingdom of Sumbria. It remained stationary, holding the gem, and was surrounded by many guards. Gilberto Ilego threw a makeshift bomb containing smokepowder and dust of darkness towards the golem, in an attempt to steal the Sun Gem''. However, in the darkness, it was actually the firebird Tekoriikii who stole the gem from right out of the golem's grasp.

The golem lost control of its actions, and moved erratically through the field of darkness while a guard who had climbed on top of it was trying to catch Tekoriikii. But the cheeky firebird evaded all this and escaped with the diamond.
